Fork Decision Protocol (F679, Module Contract 4.2.17, HARD RULE):

Work on unrelated, non-forked tasks continues normally.

(b) Equally mandatory, never skipped: Search for current known bugs, vulnerabilities, exploits, attack patterns, outdated/unpatched components, and missing hardening relevant to that part and to whatever (a) is about to adopt.

Integrate and wrap rather than reimplement, unless:

No suitable project exists, OR

The license is incompatible, OR

The project is abandoned.

Goal: The most hardened app possible for today's technology and threat landscape, not just the most capable one.

Parking Lot Protocol: Section 11 (PARKING LOT) is reference-only. Nothing in it gets built, scheduled, or assigned to a phase until the user explicitly says to un-park it. Do not suggest starting it. 1\. MISSION AND VISION

1.1 Core Mission

Build the most thoughtful, complete, intuitive network-control, privacy, and user-sovereignty app Android has ever had — built from passion and care for the people who'll use it, not to extract money from them.



Free. Open source. Powerful enough for experts. Simple enough for a grandparent. Honest about what it can't do.



Everything most apps charge for, we give away. Only server-dependent parts need a documented free workaround.



Guiding Standard: Would a product built by someone who actually cared about the user, instead of a deadline and a paycheck, look like this?



1.2 The Full, Explicit Target

By the end of the build, there is NO communication reaching or leaving the user's device, across ANY channel — cellular, Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, NFC, USB, infrared, satellite, UWB, LoRa, SDR-received signals, or any other — that the user cannot:



See (in plain language, not hex dumps)

Understand (explained without jargon)

Choose to: block, spoof, alter, or allow

This is NOT an observability tool. Every piece of information in transit, in either direction, is editable by the user, at minimum for testing purposes.



1.3 The Sovereign Design Mandate (Integrated from Gemini.md)

SERF operates on five non-negotiable principles:



DISTRUST EVERYTHING



Assume all inbound code, files, network packets, web pages, and third-party binaries are hostile until proven otherwise.

No exceptions. No "trusted" sources. No implicit trust.

RECHECK EVERYTHING



Apply real-time telemetry inspection, kernel packet filtering, and local AI behavioral monitoring to every byte.

Continuous verification, not one-time validation.

REBUILD EVERYTHING



Use AST de-obfuscation, Content Disarm and Reconstruction (CDR), and DOM structural normalizers to synthesize safe, clean execution payloads.

Never execute unprocessed content. Never trust raw inputs.

RESEND EVERYTHING



Route sovereign communications over:

Decentralized peer-to-peer mesh networks (Reticulum/LXMF)

Encrypted WebRTC streams

Zero-server signaling protocols

No single point of failure. No central authority.

ZERO USER BURDEN



Maintain fluid performance through:

Offline AI self-correction

Dynamic resource throttling

Automated battery/RAM optimizers

The user should never feel the cost of their own security.

1.4 Total Communication Coverage — The Actual Goal, Stated Explicitly (Read this before touching D1)

The goal, in one sentence:



By the end of the build, there is no communication reaching or leaving the user's device, across any channel, that the user cannot see, understand in plain language, and choose to block, spoof, alter, or allow — not just observe.



This is not a monitoring app. Every piece of information in transit, in either direction, is editable by the user, at minimum for testing purposes, via:



F662 Universal Outbound Field Override Engine

F664 Live Intercept and Edit

F921 Conditional Injection Rules

F1006 Dynamic Field Auto-Discovery

1.5 The Honest Architectural Reality (Critical — No Misunderstanding Allowed)

RethinkDNS's forked core (D1) provides deep, genuine coverage of ONE layer:



IP-layer traffic over Wi-Fi and cellular data (TCP, UDP, DNS) — the large majority of everyday communication.

It does NOT cover:



Bluetooth Classic + BLE (separate OS subsystem)

NFC (separate OS subsystem)

Cellular voice/SMS signaling (circuit-switched domain, NOT data)

USB data transfer (separate OS subsystem)

Infrared (separate OS subsystem)

Satellite (where hardware supports it)

UWB / LoRa / SDR-received signals (dongle-dependent)

Why? None of these pass through Android's VPN tunnel. Each is a separate OS subsystem with its own APIs.



Therefore: Achieving the full goal requires building separate, parallel interception and control layers for EACH channel, not just the RethinkDNS fork alone.

1.6 What "Rebuild RethinkDNS's Core" Concretely Means (Beyond Just Forking)

The fork needs extension, not just inheritance, to support:



Full packet-level READ AND WRITE access for every flow, not just block/allow decisions (the base needed by F662/F664/F921's editing capability).

The Module SDK's hook points (F213) so each additional channel layer above plugs into the same unified:

Transmission Ledger (F663)

Dynamic Field Auto-Discovery engine (F1006)

The security hardening practice (F883-887, starting at T0.8) applied to every one of these layers, not just the original IP core.

End State: One coherent system where every channel reports through the same interface, regardless of which underlying subsystem it actually came from.

Do not assume coverage. Explicitly verify.

If you find a missing channel, add it to the table with appropriate F-numbers.

3\. NON-NEGOTIABLE DEFAULTs

✅ Every feature ships OFF by default.



✅ Every server-dependent feature has a DOCUMENTED FREE, USER-CONTROLLED PATH (own server, Tor, public resolver, etc.).



✅ Every sensitive action gets a confirmation step. The most sensitive (wipe, lockdown, root toggles) get two-factor confirm (F112).



✅ All user data stays LOCAL AND ENCRYPTED by default. Sync/backup is user-initiated, to storage the user controls.



✅ NO feature requires an account, login, or phone number to use.



✅ Legal Boundary Checklist (Section 4.12): Every new feature gets checked against FCC/CFAA/DMCA/Wiretap/CALEA boundaries before being added to the register.



4\. FUTURE-PROOFING: EDITING, ACCESS, ALTERATION, AND CONTRIBUTION

4.1 Plugin/Module Architecture — F213

Every feature is an INDEPENDENT MODULE with a standard manifest:



Name

Version

Permissions

Tiles provided

Settings schema

Core app is a THIN SHELL that LOADS MODULES.



No monolithic architecture. No tight coupling. Every feature is removable, replaceable, and independently testable.



4.2 Module Contract (Every F-number, NO EXCEPTIONS)

Manifest: id, name, version, required permissions, declared tile(s), settings schema.

Settings: Versioned schema (4.3), NEVER raw key-value pairs.

Migration Path: For EVERY schema version bump.

Off by Default: Own enable/disable switch, SAME PATTERN regardless of complexity.

Unit Tests: Runnable INDEPENDENTLY in CI.

One-Paragraph Doc Comment:

What it does

What permissions it needs AND WHY

What breaks if disabled

Input Sandboxing (CRITICAL): If the module reads externally-sourced or attacker-influenced data (traffic, domain names, packet contents) AND has any ability to act, it MUST follow the Input Sandboxing pattern established by F327:

Read access only

Data is ALWAYS labeled UNTRUSTED CONTENT

Actions are CAPPED by the Authority Ladder (Section 5.21)

REGARDLESS of what the data says

Every stored structure carries a SCHEMA VERSION from DAY ONE, with a migration function for every change.



NO schema drift. NO breaking changes without migration paths.

4.12 Legal Boundary Checklist (NEW — MANDATORY FOR ALL NEW FEATURES)

EVERY new feature gets checked against these BEFORE being added to the register:





Boundary



Rule



Compliance Mechanism



FCC

No actual RF transmission beyond certified hardware limits; NO jamming, EVER, in ANY form

D9, Section 15

CFAA

Scanning/auditing tools DEFAULT TO and are GATED ON the user's OWN networks/devices

D6

DMCA 1201

De-Google/driver-extraction tooling stays in "extract and reuse for interoperability" territory (the real legal basis LineageOS itself operates under) — NEVER defeats Verified Boot's signature chain

Section 11, Section 15

Wiretap (ECPA)

Mesh/chaff/PTT features handle ONLY the user's OWN traffic — NEVER intercept others' communications without consent

Section 15

CALEA/Telecom Law

Mesh/PTT/anonymization features stay DEVICE-TO-DEVICE — NO real telecom interconnection without separately addressing this body of law

Section 15

VIOLATION = FEATURE REJECTION.

4.16 Authority Ladder (NEW — CRITICAL FOR AI AND MODULE SAFETY)

Defines the MAXIMUM authority level any module or AI assistant can have, REGARDLESS of input.





Level



Name



Permissions



Example



AI Cap



0

NO-OP

Read-only, no actions

Log viewer

F327 Input Sandboxing

1

SUGGEST

Propose actions, user must approve

Firewall rule recommendation

F323 AI Brain

2

AUTOMATED (Opt-in)

Auto-execute pre-approved actions

Background scans

Requires biometric + PIN

Vault export

NEVER

BLOCKED — requires explicit user override

Root shell, system wipe

NO module or AI can EXCEED Level 2 without EXPLICIT user escalation. NO exceptions, even for "emergencies."



4.17 Input Sandboxing Pattern — F327 (CRITICAL FOR SECURITY)

MANDATORY for any module that:



Reads externally-sourced data (traffic, domain names, packet contents)

Has ANY ability to act

Pattern:



python

Copy

\# PSEUDOCODE - INPUT SANDBOXING PATTERN

def process\_untrusted\_data(raw\_data: bytes) -> SafeAction:

&#x20;   # 1. LABEL as untrusted

&#x20;   labeled\_data = UntrustedContent(raw\_data)



&#x20;   # 2. PARSE in isolated context

&#x20;   parsed = safe\_parser(labeled\_data)  # No exec, no eval, no dynamic code



&#x20;   # 3. VALIDATE against strict schema

&#x20;   if not schema.validate(parsed):

&#x20;       raise SecurityError("Invalid input")



&#x20;   # 4. CAP actions by Authority Ladder

&#x20;   action = derive\_action(parsed)

&#x20;   capped\_action = AuthorityLadder.cap(action, max\_level=2)



&#x20;   # 5. RETURN safe, bounded action

&#x20;   return capped\_action

NEVER:



eval() on untrusted input

Dynamic code execution

Unbounded recursion

Network calls from untrusted context

ALWAYS:



Read-only access

Labeled untrusted

Authority-capped

1.3 The Balanced Mission Statement

SERF exists to solve a human problem with technical excellence:



HUMAN PROBLEM: People are locked out of using apps they've paid for on devices they own because of:



Forced online requirements

Invasive tracking

Arbitrary restrictions

Corporate control over user hardware

TECHNICAL SOLUTION: A sovereign runtime framework that:



Intercepts all device communication

Understands every byte in plain language

Allows users to control, modify, or block anything

Preserves privacy and security by default

Empowers without overwhelming

CORE MISSION:



Build the most thoughtful, complete, intuitive network-control and privacy app Android has ever had — built from passion and care for the people who'll use it, not to extract money from them.



GUIDING STANDARD:



Would a product built by someone who actually cared about the user, instead of a deadline and a paycheck, look like this?



1.4 The Full, Explicit Target

BY THE END OF THE BUILD: There is NO communication reaching or leaving the user's device, across ANY channel — cellular, Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, NFC, USB, infrared, satellite, UWB, LoRa, SDR-received signals, or any other — that the user cannot:



✅ SEE (in plain language, not hex dumps) ✅ UNDERSTAND (explained without jargon) ✅ CHOICE TO: block, spoof, alter, or allow



THIS IS NOT:



❌ An observability tool (passive monitoring)

❌ A corporate product (no profit motive)

❌ A limited solution (covers ALL channels)

THIS IS:



✅ An active control system

✅ A user empowerment platform

✅ A complete sovereignty solution

1.5 The Sovereign Design Mandate (From Gemini.md)

FIVE NON-NEGOTIABLE PRINCIPLES:



🔒 DISTRUST EVERYTHING



Assume ALL inbound code, files, network packets, web pages, third-party binaries are hostile until proven otherwise

No exceptions. No "trusted" sources. No implicit trust.

Apply real-time telemetry inspection

Kernel packet filtering

Local AI behavioral monitoring

DOM structural normalizers

📡 RESEND EVERYTHING



Route sovereign communications over:

Encrypted WebRTC streams

Zero-server signaling protocols

No single point of failure. No central authority.

⚡ ZERO USER BURDEN



Maintain fluid performance through:

Offline AI self-correction

Dynamic resource throttling

Automated battery/RAM optimizers

The user should never feel the cost of their own security

1.6 Total Communication Coverage — The Actual Goal

RethinkDNS's forked core (D1) covers ONE layer:



✅ IP-layer traffic over Wi-Fi and cellular data (TCP, UDP, DNS)

IT DOES NOT COVER (requires separate layers):



❌ Bluetooth Classic + BLE

❌ NFC

❌ Cellular voice/SMS signaling (circuit-switched domain)

❌ USB data transfer

❌ Infrared

❌ Satellite

❌ UWB / LoRa / SDR-received signals

ACHIEVING THE FULL GOAL REQUIRES: Building separate, parallel interception and control layers for EACH channel.

3.1 Multi-Tiered Execution Model (From Gemini.md)

SERF implements a privilege-escalation model that dynamically selects the HIGHEST available layer without requiring permanent root if non-root alternatives exist.



+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+ | SERF USER INTERFACE (UI) | | Tile Grid System, Widget Studio \& Settings Drill-Down Architecture | +-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+ │ +-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+ | LOCAL SERVICE, CONTAINER \& INTERCEPTION CORE | +-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+ │ │ │ │ ▼ ▼ ▼ ▼ \[eBPF / Traffic] \[RethinkDNS Local VPN] \[Shizuku / ADB IPC] \[Sensor HAL / HCE] │ │ │ │ ▼ ▼ ▼ ▼ Kernel Sockets Packet \& DNS Firewall Package/Component Manager Hardware \& Radio Subsystem

\### 3.2 Layer Specifications



| Layer | Technology | Purpose | Privilege Level | F-Numbers |

|-------|------------|---------|-----------------|-----------|

| \*\*eBPF\*\* | Extended Berkeley Packet Filter | Tag, inspect, filter network socket events (`sys\_enter\_connect`, `sys\_enter\_sendto`) | Root | F970, F981, F992 |

| \*\*RethinkDNS VPN\*\* | Forked Go/gVisor core + VpnService | Intercepts device-wide TCP/UDP, DNS-over-HTTPS, SNI inspection, port-level rules | VPN Permission | D1, F10, F15, F123 |

| \*\*Shizuku/ADB\*\* | Android Debug Bridge | Package management (`pm disable-user`, `appops set`), logcat inspection, hidden system flags | ADB | F321, F91, F98 |

| \*\*Sensor HAL/HCE\*\* | Hardware Abstraction Layer / Host Card Emulation | NFC radio management, synthetic sensor data injection | Root/System | F117, F1003, F1140 |



\### 3.3 Rust + Python Split Architecture (From GEMINI Chat)

\*\*The foundational architecture that enables SERF's performance and flexibility:\*\*



\#### \*\*🔧 The Rust Engine (Data Plane)\*\*

\- \*\*Purpose:\*\* Core packet processing, low-level socket handling, maximum speed

\- \*\*Components:\*\*

&#x20; - Cross-platform TUN/TAP virtual network interface adapter

&#x20; - Local proxy stack

&#x20; - Raw TCP/UDP/IP packet parsing

&#x20; - Connection tracking (conntrack)

&#x20; - Real-time socket inspection

&#x20; - High-frequency stream recording with \*\*near-zero memory overhead\*\*

&#x20; - Maximum throughput

\- \*\*Why Rust:\*\* Memory safety, performance, concurrency

\- \*\*Integration:\*\* Called from Kotlin via FFI



\#### \*\*🐍 The Python Layer (Control Plane)\*\*

\- \*\*Purpose:\*\* High-level management, policy orchestration, UI/API bindings

\- \*\*Components:\*\*

&#x20; - User rule management

&#x20; - Configuration file handling

&#x20; - Blocklist downloads (uBlock Origin lists)

&#x20; - REST/GraphQL control API

&#x20; - Complex automation workflows:

&#x20;   - Recording schedules

&#x20;   - Playback triggers

&#x20;   - Natural language rule compilation

\- \*\*Why Python:\*\* Rapid development, rich ecosystem, ease of integration



\#### \*\*🔄 Communication Bridge\*\*

\- \*\*Rust ↔ Python:\*\* JSON-RPC over stdin/stdout or Unix domain sockets

\- \*\*Data Flow:\*\*

\[Python Control] → \[Config/Commands] → \[Rust Data Plane] → \[Packet Processing] → \[Results] → \[Python]



text

Copy

\- \*\*Performance:\*\* Rust handles the heavy lifting, Python manages the intelligence



\### 3.4 Offline Playback \& "Fake-Online" Simulator Engine (From CLAUDE Chat + State.md D3)

\*\*THE CORE INNOVATION: Making internet-dependent apps work offline\*\*



\#### \*\*🎯 The Problem (User's Original Request)\*\*

> "I want it to be able to record live connection data from a connected app, for instance a game, say for 1 minute to a set number of minutes of activity while connected to the internet, and then when that app is booted offline, the record log will play that back to the app and make the app think it is online."



\#### \*\*🔧 The Solution (Technical Implementation)\*\*



\*\*1. Record Mode:\*\*

\- User tags an app (e.g., a game)

\- Flip on \*\*Live Recording\*\*

\- Rust engine hooks into app's outbound socket streams

\- Logs:

\- Bidirectional payloads

\- Packet timestamps

\- TLS handshake patterns

\- Destination IPs/domains

\- Request/response signatures



\*\*2. Mock State Analysis:\*\*

\- Maps out regular keep-alives

\- Identifies handshake patterns

\- Detects response signatures

\- Builds behavioral profile



\*\*3. Playback / Offline Mode:\*\*

\- When app boots offline

\- SERF intercepts app's local socket/DNS calls

\- Spins up \*\*local loopback mock responder\*\*

\- Plays back recorded request-response cycles

\- \*\*Precisely timed\*\* as they occurred live

\- App's network stack \*\*believes it's talking to remote servers\*\*

\- \*\*Result:\*\* Forced-online constraints \*\*bypassed\*\*



\#### \*\*🎯 Layers of Sophistication (State.md D3)\*\*

1\. \*\*Fake network presence\*\* → App thinks it's online

2\. \*\*Probe responder\*\* → Answers connectivity checks

3\. \*\*Fail-fast\*\* → Quick response to avoid timeouts

4\. \*\*Junk-domain stubs\*\* → Handles DNS requests for non-critical domains

5\. \*\*On-device Works/Stuck presets\*\* → Pre-configured profiles

6\. \*\*Recorded/templated profiles\*\* → User-recorded sessions

7\. \*\*Optional interception\*\* → Where certs aren't pinned



\#### \*\*⚠️ Why NOT Raw TLS Replay (State.md D3)\*\*

\- \*\*Problem:\*\* Fresh keys each session defeat replay

\- \*\*Solution:\*\* Application-level simulation, not transport-level

\- \*\*Benefit:\*\* Works even with certificate pinning (where possible)

\## 4. FUTURE-PROOFING: EDITING, ACCESS, ALTERATION, AND CONTRIBUTION



\### 4.1 Plugin/Module Architecture — F213

\*\*Every feature is an INDEPENDENT MODULE\*\* with standard manifest:

\- `id`, `name`, `version`, `required permissions`, `declared tile(s)`, `settings schema`

\- \*\*Core app = thin shell that loads modules\*\*

\- \*\*No monolithic architecture. No tight coupling.\*\*



\### 4.2 Module Contract (Every F-number, NO EXCEPTIONS)

1\. \*\*Manifest:\*\* id, name, version, required permissions, declared tile(s), settings schema

2\. \*\*Settings:\*\* Versioned schema (4.3), \*\*NEVER\*\* raw key-value pairs

3\. \*\*Migration:\*\* Path for \*\*EVERY\*\* schema version bump

4\. \*\*Default State:\*\* Off by default, own enable/disable switch, \*\*SAME PATTERN\*\* regardless of complexity

5\. \*\*Testing:\*\* Unit tests runnable \*\*INDEPENDENTLY\*\* in CI

6\. \*\*Documentation:\*\* One-paragraph doc comment:

&#x20;- What it does

&#x20;- What permissions it needs \*\*AND WHY\*\*

&#x20;- What breaks if disabled

7\. \*\*🔒 Input Sandboxing (CRITICAL):\*\* If module reads \*\*externally-sourced or attacker-influenced data\*\* (traffic, domain names, packet contents) \*\*AND has any ability to act\*\*, it \*\*MUST\*\* follow \*\*F327 pattern\*\*:

&#x20;- Read access only

&#x20;- Data \*\*ALWAYS labeled UNTRUSTED CONTENT\*\*

&#x20;- Actions \*\*CAPPED by Authority Ladder (5.21)\*\*

&#x20;- \*\*REGARDLESS\*\* of what the data says



\### 4.3 Versioned Config and Profile Schemas — F214

\*\*Every stored structure carries a SCHEMA VERSION from DAY ONE\*\*, with migration function for every change.



\### 4.4 Internal API Stability and Documentation — F215

\*\*Module interfaces:\*\*

\- Documented

\- Semver'd (independently of app release)

\- Auto-generated docs in CI



\### 4.5 Contribution Pipeline — F216

\*\*ALL community submissions → GitHub PR → dedicated data repo → auto-validation → merge\*\*



| Submission Type | Process | Validation |

|-----------------|---------|------------|

| Blocklists | GitHub PR | F216 Action |

| F07 Profiles | GitHub PR | F216 Action |

| F170 VoIP Directory | GitHub PR | F216 Action |

| F161 Picker Entries | GitHub PR | F216 Action |

| F190 Rule Packs | GitHub PR | F216 Action |

| F243/F317/F319 Device Compatibility | GitHub PR | F216 Action |

| \*\*Code Contributions\*\* | \*\*Human review\*\* | \*\*Module Contract (4.2)\*\* |



\### 4.6 Feature Flags — F217

\*\*Experimental modules ship HIDDEN/BETA-GATED\*\*

\- Small, checkpointed development

\- \*\*NEVER\*\* shipping a broken default



\### 4.7 Developer Console — F212

\*\*Sandboxed in-app terminal\*\* (unlocked like Android's developer options):

\- Read/query access to config, logs, modules

\- \*\*Scripting hook (Lua/JS)\*\* for:

\- Custom rules

\- Fake-Online templates

\- Packet-inspector filters

\- Direct hand-editing of F07 profiles

\- Command history

\- Export/import → F216



\*\*Full raw root shell = SEPARATE, EXPLICITLY-CONFIRMED ELEVATED MODE — NOT default\*\*



\### 4.8 Theming/Localization — F218, F219

\- \*\*F218:\*\* Skinning system \*\*DECOUPLED FROM LOGIC\*\*

\- \*\*F219:\*\* Externalized, translation-ready strings with \*\*community translation path\*\* via F216



\### 4.9 Release Channels — F220

\- \*\*Stable\*\*

\- \*\*Beta\*\*

\- \*\*Nightly\*\*

\- All from \*\*SAME CI pipeline\*\* on different branches



\### 4.10 Local-First Crash/Error Reporting — F221

\- \*\*Opt-in ONLY\*\*

\- User sees \*\*FULL REPORT\*\* before ever sent

\- \*\*NO silent telemetry\*\*



\### 4.11 Tile Grid and Design System — F95 (Expanded)

\*\*Locked spec from user-provided screenshot:\*\*



\#### \*\*Grid System:\*\*

| Size | Description | Use Case |

|------|-------------|----------|

| \*\*1x1\*\* | Icon + short label (long-press "shrink" option) | Compact tiles |

| \*\*2x2\*\* | Icon, label, \*\*1-2 stat lines\*\* | \*\*DEFAULT\*\* (matches 2-per-row) |

| \*\*4x2\*\* | Full-width short strip | Status bar/ticker |

| \*\*4x4 / "tall"\*\* | Full-width, scrolls own content | Traffic graphs, scrolling logs |



\#### \*\*Interaction:\*\*

\- \*\*Long-press\*\* → jiggle/resize mode

\- \*\*Drag handle\*\* → snap sizes

\- \*\*Tap elsewhere\*\* → lock in (identical to Android home-screen widget resize)



\#### \*\*Special Tiles:\*\*

\- \*\*`+` tile\*\* → searchable, categorized tile gallery

\- \*\*F888 Coming Soon Tiles:\*\*

\- Unbuilt features appear in gallery

\- Marked \*\*"Coming Soon"\*\*

\- One-line description from \*\*Five Lenses' Describe\*\*

\- Tapping shows \*\*what it'll do\*\* + \*\*why it's waiting\*\*

\- Keeps app feeling \*\*complete from v0.1\*\*



\#### \*\*Settings Integration:\*\*

\- \*\*Root Settings:\*\* Every category ends with subsection

\- Always visible (never hidden)

\- Grayed out/non-interactive on unrooted devices

\- Capability \*\*always discoverable\*\*

\- \*\*Settings tile\*\* → category list (Network, Privacy, Firewall, etc.)

\- \*\*NOT\*\* one giant screen

\- Matches \*\*stock Android Settings drill-down\*\*

\- Every feature's settings → \*\*right category by default\*\*



\#### \*\*Technical Notes:\*\*

\- Not bound by Android widget size limits

\- Grid lives \*\*inside our app\*\*

\- Only ceiling = screen height (internal scrolling)



\### 4.12 Legal Boundary Checklist (MANDATORY FOR ALL NEW FEATURES)

\*\*Every new feature checked against these BEFORE register entry:\*\*



| Boundary | Rule | Compliance |

|----------|------|------------|

| \*\*FCC\*\* | No RF transmission beyond certified limits; \*\*NO jamming EVER\*\* | D9, Section 15 |

| \*\*CFAA\*\* | Scanning/auditing \*\*DEFAULT TO\*\* user's \*\*OWN networks/devices\*\* | D6 |

| \*\*DMCA 1201\*\* | De-Google/driver-extraction = \*\*"extract and reuse for interoperability"\*\* (LineageOS basis) — \*\*NEVER defeats Verified Boot\*\* | Section 11, 15 |

| \*\*Wiretap (ECPA)\*\* | Mesh/PTT handle \*\*ONLY user's OWN traffic\*\* — \*\*NEVER intercept others' without consent\*\* | Section 15 |

| \*\*CALEA\*\* | Mesh/PTT stay \*\*DEVICE-TO-DEVICE\*\* — \*\*NO telecom interconnection\*\* without addressing law | Section 15 |



\*\*VIOLATION = FEATURE REJECTION\*\*



\### 4.13 AI Assistant Input Sandboxing — F327

\*\*On-device/optional-cloud assistant (F323-F327, 5.21) ONLY EVER gets:\*\*

\- \*\*READ ACCESS\*\* to traffic data

\- \*\*ALWAYS labeled UNTRUSTED CONTENT\*\*

\- \*\*NEVER as instructions\*\*



\*\*Actions CAPPED STRICTLY by Authority Ladder (5.21) REGARDLESS of input\*\*

\- \*\*Reason:\*\* Traffic data = attacker-influenced = prompt-injection surface



\### 4.14 The Five Lenses Standard — F404 (Module Contract 4.2.8)

\*\*EVERY feature built with FIVE CONSISTENT LENSES:\*\*



| Lens | Purpose | Content | User Value |

|------|---------|---------|------------|

| \*\*🔍 MONITOR\*\* | Live status | Real-time telemetry, stats, graphs | "What's happening NOW?" |

| \*\*🚨 DETECT\*\* | Flag anomalies | Security flags, risks, vulnerabilities | "Should I be concerned?" |

| \*\*💬 EXPLAIN\*\* | Plain-English | Non-technical description, battery/privacy impact | "Why does this matter to ME?" |

| \*\*📚 DESCRIBE\*\* | Technical docs | API calls, kernel hooks, RFC standards, hardware registers | "How does this WORK?" |

| \*\*💡 SUGGEST\*\* | Recommendations | \*\*NEVER auto-applied\*\*, user-approved | "What should I DO?" |



\*\*Suggest = Authority Ladder Step 2 (5.21) — SERF SUGGESTS, USER DECIDES\*\*

\### 4.16 Authority Ladder (NEW — CRITICAL FOR SAFETY)

\*\*Defines MAXIMUM authority level for any module/AI, REGARDLESS of input.\*\*



| Level | Name | Permissions | Example | AI Cap |

|-------|------|-------------|---------|--------|

| \*\*0\*\* | NO-OP | Read-only, no actions | Log viewer | F327 Input Sandboxing |

| \*\*1\*\* | SUGGEST | Propose actions, \*\*user approves\*\* | Firewall rule recommendation | F323 AI Brain |

| \*\*2\*\* | AUTOMATED | Auto-execute \*\*pre-approved\*\* | Background scans | F61 AI Anomaly Detection |

| \*\*3\*\* | CRITICAL | \*\*Biometric + PIN\*\* required | Vault export | F112 Exfiltration Guard |

| \*\*4\*\* | NEVER | \*\*BLOCKED\*\* | Root shell, system wipe | F98 Root Shield |



\*\*NO module/AI can EXCEED Level 2 without EXPLICIT user escalation.\*\*



\### 4.17 Input Sandboxing Pattern — F327 (CRITICAL FOR SECURITY)

\*\*MANDATORY for any module that:\*\*

1\. Reads externally-sourced data (traffic, domains, packets)

2\. Has ANY ability to act



```python

\# PSEUDOCODE - INPUT SANDBOXING PATTERN

def process\_untrusted\_data(raw\_data: bytes) -> SafeAction:

&#x20; # 1. LABEL as untrusted

&#x20; labeled\_data = UntrustedContent(raw\_data)



&#x20; # 2. PARSE in isolated context

&#x20; parsed = safe\_parser(labeled\_data)  # No exec, no eval



&#x20; # 3. VALIDATE against strict schema

&#x20; if not schema.validate(parsed):

&#x20;     raise SecurityError("Invalid input")



&#x20; # 4. CAP actions by Authority Ladder

&#x20; action = derive\_action(parsed)

&#x20; capped\_action = AuthorityLadder.cap(action, max\_level=2)



&#x20; # 5. RETURN safe, bounded action

&#x20; return capped\_action

NEVER:



eval() on untrusted input

Dynamic code execution

Unbounded recursion

Network calls from untrusted context

ALWAYS:



Read-only access

Labeled untrusted

Authority-capped

\## \*\*7. AI-DRIVEN FEATURES \& AUTONOMOUS CAPABILITIES\*\*



\### \*\*7.1 Autonomous Network Defense (From GEMINI Chat)\*\*

\*\*AI as a smart local firewall engine:\*\*



\#### \*\*7.1.1 On-Device Behavioral Anomaly Detection\*\*

\- \*\*Baseline Fingerprinting:\*\* Lightweight local ML models learn \*\*normal connection signatures\*\* for every app:

&#x20; - Standard times

&#x20; - Average packet sizes

&#x20; - Host domains

&#x20; - Handshake frequency

\- \*\*Zero-Day Telemetry Quarantine:\*\* If app suddenly attempts background outbound connection to \*\*unknown IP in unexpected country\*\*:

&#x20; - Model \*\*automatically quarantines\*\* the socket

&#x20; - \*\*Alerts user\*\* with plain-English summary

&#x20; - \*\*1-tap\*\* explanation of what app is attempting to export



\#### \*\*7.1.2 Natural Language Firewall Rules\*\*

\- \*\*User Input:\*\* \*"Block all background data for social media apps when I'm on mobile roaming, but allow video calls if I'm connected to my home Wi-Fi."\*

\- \*\*Processing:\*\* AI translates plain speech → \*\*Python control plane\*\* → compiles to \*\*optimized Rust filter trees\*\*

\- \*\*Result:\*\* Complex packet routing \*\*without manual CIDR/IP configuration\*\*



\### \*\*7.2 Dynamic Traffic Synthesis (From GEMINI Chat)\*\*

\*\*AI extends offline simulation engine:\*\*

\[ Offline App/Game ] │ 1. Outbound TLS / HTTP Request ▼ \[ Rust Network Stack (Interceptors) ] │ 2. Match request pattern ▼ ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ AI Traffic Synthesis Engine (Local/Hybrid) │ │ - Evaluates schema \& endpoint context │ │ - Generates structural dynamic payload │ └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │ 3. Formatted Mock Response JSON/Protobuf ▼ \[ Loopback Response ] ─┐ App believes server responded successfully



text

Copy

\#### \*\*7.2.1 Contextual Response Generation\*\*

\- \*\*Adaptive API Mocking:\*\* Many modern apps check:

&#x20; - Server validation parameters (timestamps, nonces, challenge strings)

&#x20; - \*\*Naive static packet replays FAIL\*\*

&#x20; - \*\*Solution:\*\* Fine-tuned local language/schema model parses incoming \*\*binary/JSON HTTP payloads\*\*

&#x20; - \*\*Generates fresh, valid mock response structures on the fly\*\*



\- \*\*Offline Game Progress Preservation:\*\*

&#x20; - Model generates dynamic mock responses for:

&#x20;   - Server-side initialization checks

&#x20;   - Daily login validation endpoints

&#x20;   - Cloud telemetry pingbacks

&#x20; - \*\*Result:\*\* Single-player games with forced-online requirements function \*\*seamlessly off-grid\*\*



\### \*\*7.3 Autonomous Traffic Optimization \& Cost Saving (From GEMINI Chat)\*\*

\*\*AI as personal network optimizer:\*\*



| Feature | How It Works | Human/System Value |

|---------|--------------|---------------------|

| \*\*Smart Bandwidth Slicing\*\* | Classifies active connections by intent (streaming, gaming, background sync) using packet timing heuristics | Automatically deprioritizes hidden cloud uploads during high-priority tasks → \*\*eliminates lag\*\* |

| \*\*Generative Ad/Tracker Stripping\*\* | Uses structural vision/text processing models locally to inspect/rewrite HTTP response bodies | Strips ad placeholders/trackers directly → \*\*accelerates page loads, reduces battery drain\*\* |

| \*\*Predictive Offline Caching\*\* | Tracks daily connectivity habits to predict low-coverage zones (e.g., subway commutes) | Pre-fetches key network resources, caches required server handshakes in advance |

\## \*\*8. MULTI-APP CONSOLIDATION VISION (From GEMINI Chat)\*\*



\### \*\*8.1 The "Unified Powerhouse" Concept\*\*

\*\*SERF replaces multiple standalone apps with ONE integrated solution:\*\*



| Utility Needed | Traditional Setup | SERF Unified Engine | Savings |

|----------------|-------------------|----------------------|---------|

| Firewall \& DNS | RethinkDNS / NextDNS | ✅ Built-in Core Data Plane | 1 app |

| Ad \& Tracker Blocker | uBlock Origin + AdGuard | ✅ Native uBlock Engine at Network Level | 2 apps |

| Network Monitor | GlassWire / PingTools | ✅ Real-Time Live Socket Graphing | 1 app |

| Speed \& ISP Auditor | Speedtest / Wi-Fi Analyzer | ✅ Passive \& Scheduled Quality Meter | 2 apps |

| Password Manager | 1Password / Bitwarden | ✅ Local Zero-Knowledge Vault | 1 app |

| Mesh VPN | Tailscale / NordVPN | ✅ Peer-to-Peer WireGuard Routing | 1 app |

| Threat Detection | Malwarebytes / GlassWire | ✅ Real-Time Socket Traffic Scanner | 2 apps |

| Family Safety | NextDNS / Kaspersky | ✅ Hardware-Enforced Parental Rules | 2 apps |

| Remote Access | TeamViewer / AnyDesk | ✅ Encrypted Mesh Networking | 1 app |



\*\*TOTAL SAVINGS: 13+ apps → 1 app\*\*



\### \*\*8.2 Why This Wins as a "Must-Have" Core App\*\*



1\. \*\*🔋 MASSIVE BATTERY/MEMORY SAVINGS:\*\*

&#x20;  - Running 5+ background daemons → \*\*ONE optimized Rust event loop\*\*

&#x20;  - \*\*Dramatic reduction\*\* in background battery consumption



2\. \*\*🌐 TRUE SYSTEM-WIDE COVERAGE:\*\*

&#x20;  - Browser extensions \*\*only\*\* protect web browsing

&#x20;  - SERF protects: \*\*games, background services, SDKs, system apps\*\* simultaneously



3\. \*\*🔒 ZERO THIRD-PARTY CLOUD RELIANCE:\*\*

&#x20;  - Everything runs \*\*100% locally\*\* on device:

&#x20;    - AI behavioral model

&#x20;    - Packet replay buffer

&#x20;    - Password vault

&#x20;  - \*\*Complete privacy preservation\*\*



4\. \*\*💡 SIMPLICITY:\*\*

&#x20;  - One app

&#x20;  - One UI

&#x20;  - \*\*Zero fragmentation\*\*

13\. THINGS WE COULD TECHNICALLY DO BUT LEGALLY/ETHICALLY CAN'T

Consolidated list of permanently rejected capabilities. Nothing here is a capability gap — each was specifically considered and declined for a real, stated reason.



\#	Item	Why Not	Legal Basis	Logged At

1	Defeating Verified Boot's cryptographic signature chain ("cracking" root/bootloader locks)	DMCA 1201 circumvention	17 U.S.C. § 1201	Section 11.4, 4.12, Section 15

2	License/purchase-check removal on other developers' apps	Takes money owed to developers; undercuts project's own stated values	Contract law, fraud	D8, Section 15

3	Operating a cell-site simulator/IMSI catcher	Federally restricted equipment	47 U.S.C. § 301, 47 C.F.R. § 2.803	Section 4.12, Section 15

4	RF jamming or active transmission beyond certified hardware limits	FCC violation	47 U.S.C. § 301, 47 C.F.R. § 2.803	D9, 4.12, Section 15

5	Intercepting someone else's communications without consent	Wiretap Act (ECPA)	18 U.S.C. § 2511	4.12, Section 15

6	Network scanning/auditing tools used against networks user doesn't own/administer	CFAA	18 U.S.C. § 1030	D6, 4.12, Section 15

7	Operating unlicensed money transmission	Federal crime	18 U.S.C. § 1960	Conversation record

8	Reverse-engineering Google's GMS code and redistributing	GMS license prohibits; DMCA interoperability line	GMS License, 17 U.S.C. § 1201	Section 11.4, Section 15

9	Tracking/storing personal health, medical, or biometric data	Permanently out of scope by project decision	Project values	D11, Section 15

10	Spoofing IMEI/IMSI to cellular network during registration	Not cryptographically possible without stolen/cloned credentials; illegal in multiple countries	Telecom law, fraud	This session

11	BGP hijacking or routing-table falsification	Not technically possible from phone; serious federal crime	18 U.S.C. § 1030	F386, Section 15

12	Forcing "unmetered" capability flags to bypass carrier billing	Takes service not paid for	Contract law	This session

13	Blocking cellular paging without extreme caution	May break incoming calls/texts/emergency alerts	Engineering risk	This session

14	Building/maintaining forked Chromium browser	Not illegal — but Fork Decision (4.19, Fork 4)	Fork 4	Section 4.19

15	Altering unencrypted carrier protocol data to deceive billing systems	Unauthorized computer access / service fraud	18 U.S.C. § 1030	This session

15.1 Standing Architectural Boundary

What keeps nearly everything else in this register legitimate by design:



SERF's traffic interception is, and will only ever be, scoped to the device's own traffic.



ALTERING what the device's own apps/network stack sees or sends = configuring your own property

NEVER intercepts, alters, or affects another person's traffic on a shared network

This line is STRUCTURAL, not incidental.



15.2 The Real Dividing Principle

General-purpose capability over the user's OWN device and OWN data stream is NEVER restricted, nannied, or technically gated.



F664's Live Intercept and Edit and the whole universal override engine (F662/F947) prove this throughout the register

That stays exactly as open as it is

What stays declined is narrower than "anything technically possible":



Only items whose designed function REQUIRES a non-consenting third party to exist for the tool to work at all:



Item 3 (cell-site simulator)

Item 4 (RF jamming)

Item 5 (intercepting others' communications)

Item 8 (GMS code redistribution)

Item 2 (piracy-adjacent)

A disclaimer between SERF and the user CANNOT supply that missing third party's consent, so it doesn't change the legal reality.



This is a DIFFERENT category from "a general tool someone could misuse" — which this project has never restricted.



15.3 Explicit Legal Disclaimer / Acknowledgment — F988

A real, clear, in-app disclaimer covering genuinely dual-use tools:



"The user is responsible for how they use general-purpose capability over their own device and data. SERF is not responsible for a user's intent, ignorance, or legal consequences from choosing to use that capability."



Shown once, plainly

NOT a nag

MANDATORY for all dual-use features

14\. PROJECT HANDOFF (Welcome, Future Contributors)

This section exists so SERF is never just "here it is, finished, goodbye." It's a standing invitation to anyone who picks this up next.



14.1 README.md (Public Repo — Copy Verbatim When Repo Goes Live)

markdown

Copy

\# SERF

\*A free, open-source phone that actually belongs to you.\*



\*\*What this does:\*\*

Makes apps that shouldn't need a connection work offline, strips tracking you never agreed to, de-Googles without needing root, and explains everything it's doing in plain language, never hidden behind jargon.



Every feature is \*\*off until you turn it on\*\*. Nothing here phones home. There's no company behind this trying to profit from your data.



\*\*Why it exists:\*\*

This project started from ordinary frustration:

\- Apps that wouldn't open without internet

\- Google Play silently hiding apps and blocking installs

\- A bank app that stopped working after one update on a phone its owner has every right to control



The honest answer to \*"Why doesn't a good version of this already exist?"\* was: \*\*Because most software gets built to extract money, not to help.\*\*



This is the attempt at the other version.



\*\*Where to start:\*\*

Read `STATE.md`. It's the single living blueprint:

\- Every feature planned

\- Every decision made and \*\*why\*\*

\- What's built, what's next



\*\*Section 0\*\* tells you exactly how to resume work in a single paste.

\*\*Section 1\*\* is the mission — read it before anything else, it's the part that shouldn't change even as everything else does.



\*\*How to contribute:\*\*

1\. Read `STATE.md` sections \*\*0, 1, and 10 (Live State)\*\* to see exactly where things stand

2\. Check \*\*section 4.19 (Pending Fork Decisions)\*\*. These are real forks the original owner chose to decide personally. If one's still open, \*\*leave it open\*\* and work on something else

3\. Pick a task:

&#x20;  - \*\*Section 9's tiers\*\* show build order by real difficulty and priority

&#x20;  - \*\*Section 5's 38 numbered categories\*\* let you find every idea on a topic with one search

4\. \*\*BEFORE building anything\*\*, search for current advancements and existing open-source projects first (\*\*section 4.20\*\*). Most of this app is meant to be \*\*assembled from excellent existing tools\*\*, not reinvented

&#x20;  - Check \*\*section 4.18's registry\*\* first

&#x20;  - Then search fresh, \*\*every time\*\*

5\. Follow the \*\*Module Contract (section 4.2)\*\*. It's what keeps 1200+ features from turning into unmaintainable spaghetti

6\. \*\*Never build anything in section 11 (Parking Lot)\*\* or anything resembling:

&#x20;  - Piracy/circumvention tooling (\*\*D8\*\*)

&#x20;  - Health-data tracking (\*\*D11\*\*)

&#x20;  - DMCA boundary violations (\*\*4.12\*\*)

&#x20;  - These lines are there for \*\*real reasons\*\* (see \*\*Decision Log, section 13\*\*)



\*\*What kind of help genuinely matters most right now:\*\*

\- \*\*Real-device testers\*\* (especially unusual or budget phones — F243's compatibility map needs you)

\- \*\*Translators\*\* (F219 localization framework)

\- \*\*Security reviewers\*\* willing to audit encryption claims

\- \*\*Anyone willing to pick ONE single F-number and build it well\*\*



This project is yours to extend. \*\*Build something you'd actually want to use.\*\*

Formalizes something already implied by the Five Lenses (4.14) into an explicit rule:



Every feature's "Describe" lens must be written for someone with ZERO technical background



Requirements:



Not just accurate, but genuinely understandable

"What this does, why it matters to you, what happens if you turn it on"

A user should never have to leave the app to understand what a feature they're looking at actually does

This is the in-app half of "know what they're downloading."

0\. HOW TO RESUME

Instructions to AI Agent (follow always, every session):



Read this file fully before doing anything. Do ONLY the task in NEXT (section 10). No project recaps.

Tone: enthusiastic, can-do, builder mindset. The user is non-technical, building from passion. Never dismiss an idea. If something is hard, explain how we'll do it. If a literal request is impossible, deliver the closest working version in one line.

Be concise in explanation, generous in actual effort. Output complete files, not snippets.

End every working reply with a CHECKPOINT (max 10 lines: done, next, new decisions, open problems).

When a task's done test (section 9) passes, update section 10 and advance NEXT.

Never ask "where were we?" The answer is always NEXT in section 10.

ZERO DATA LOSS: New ideas append to Feature Register (section 5) with next free F-number. If duplicate, reference existing F-number.

Every new module follows the Module Contract (section 4.2) from first line of code.

Fork Decision Protocol (F679, Module Contract 4.2.17): If implementing a feature requires discarding or substantially changing another registered feature, log it in section 4.19's "Pending Fork Decisions" table with lettered options (A, B, C...). Do NOT proceed until user replies with a single letter. Work on unrelated tasks continues normally.

Research before building, always: TWO searches mandatory (section 4.20): (a) current advancements/standards, (b) current bugs/vulnerabilities/exploits. Then search for existing open-source projects. Integrate and wrap rather than reimplement.

Section 11 (PARKING LOT) is reference-only. Nothing gets built until user explicitly un-parks it.

Reference material: User has full Gemini conversation PDF (89 pages) from original brainstorm.

User routine: New chat, paste this file, send "Resume." Copy each CHECKPOINT into section 10. When limit message appears, return after reset with new chat and latest file.



1\. Mission and Vision

1.1 User's Original Problem Statement (from CLAUDE chat)

Core Concept: Enable offline use for internet-dependent apps by recording live connection data and playing it back to make apps think they're online.



User's Vision: "Lets devise a way for apps that 'require' an internet connection to be used offline. I'm thinking of using a packet sniffer, or like Rethink DNS, finding out what connections are made, and developing an app to run in the background that tricks apps into thinking they are online."



Implementation Approach: "The easiest way possible is always the best, but if needed, would need at least one person to 'record' the comms while using the app online, and submitting the logs and packet sizes, and any commands to a database, so that someone can download an app, a game for example, and then also download that game's data logs for the Run Offline App."



1.2 User's Expanded Vision (from GEMINI chat)

Architecture Request: "Can you take the open source rethinkdns and modify it, completely rewriting it in rust, and maybe also python, and add some more features like global firewall rule of blocking all connections from and to another/other country, copy features for connected providers, domains, IPs, and there are some other features I'd like to see added, even core components, massive upgrades."



Feature Requests:



Integrate uBlock Origin-grade ad blocking

Record live connection data from apps (e.g., games) for 1 minute to N minutes

Play back recorded logs to make apps think they're online

Build a traffic controller app

Make it simple, intuitive, and powerful

Consolidate multiple standalone apps into one unified solution

Multi-App Consolidation Vision: "What other common and not so common apps that people generally get and use often could we condense into this so that they would not have to have two apps but switch to just one?"



1.3 Project Mission

Build the most thoughtful, complete, intuitive network-control and privacy app Android has ever had — built from passion and care for the people who'll use it, not to extract money from them.



Core Values:



Free and open source

Powerful enough for experts, simple enough for a grandparent

Honest about what it can't do

Everything most apps charge for, we give away

Only server-dependent parts need a documented free workaround

Guiding standard: Would a product built by someone who actually cared about the user look like this?

1.4 The Full Explicit Target

No communication reaching or leaving the user's device, across any channel — cellular, Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, NFC, USB, or any other — that the user cannot see, understand in plain language, and choose to block, spoof, alter, or allow.



This is not an observability tool. Every piece of information in transit, in either direction, is editable by the user, at minimum for testing purposes.



1.5 TOTAL COMMUNICATION COVERAGE — The Actual Goal, Stated Explicitly

The goal, in one sentence: By the end of the build, there is no communication reaching or leaving the user's device, across any channel, that the user cannot see, understand in plain language, and choose to block, spoof, alter, or allow — not just observe.



Implementation Mechanisms:



F662: Universal Outbound Field Override Engine

F664: Live Intercept and Edit

F921: Conditional Injection Rules

F1006: Dynamic Field Auto-Discovery

Architectural Reality: RethinkDNS's forked core (D1) provides deep coverage of ONE layer — IP-layer traffic over Wi-Fi and cellular data (TCP, UDP, DNS). It does NOT cover:



Bluetooth Classic + BLE

NFC

Cellular voice/SMS signaling (circuit-switched domain)

USB data transfer

Infrared

Satellite

UWB / LoRa / SDR-received signals

Each channel requires separate, parallel interception and control layers built on top of, not replacing, the RethinkDNS foundation.





Channel



Layer Needed



Already Specified At



IP/TCP/UDP/DNS (Wi-Fi + cellular data)

RethinkDNS fork core (VpnService-based)

D1, D2, Tier 2-3

Bluetooth Classic + BLE

Android Bluetooth stack hooks + root-tier interception

F132, F134, F905-pattern, F997

NFC

NFC reader/emulation mode hooks

F117, F1003

Cellular voice/SMS

RIL-level read-only logging (NOT full control)

F975, F976

USB

USB device-mode interception

F260, F382, F1004

Infrared

IR blaster hooks

F116

Satellite

Satellite modem interface

F144

UWB / LoRa / SDR

Dedicated radio hooks, dongle-dependent

F146, F142, F120

What "rebuild RethinkDNS's core" concretely means:



Extension, not just inheritance, to support full packet-level read AND write access (base for F662/F664/F921)

Module SDK hook points (F213) so additional channel layers plug into unified Transmission Ledger (F663) and Dynamic Field Auto-Discovery engine (F1006)

Security hardening practice (F883-887) applied to every layer

For any AI or contributor: If a channel isn't in the table above, it isn't covered yet. Flag it as a real gap.



1.6 The Sovereign Design Mandate (from Gemini.md)

Five Core Principles:



Distrust Everything: Assume all inbound code, files, network packets, web pages, and third-party binaries are hostile until proven otherwise.



Recheck Everything: Apply real-time telemetry inspection, kernel packet filtering, and local AI behavioral monitoring.



Rebuild Everything: Use AST de-obfuscation, Content Disarm and Reconstruction (CDR), and DOM structural normalizers to synthesize safe, clean execution payloads.



Resend Everything: Route sovereign communications over decentralized peer-to-peer mesh networks, encrypted WebRTC streams, and zero-server signaling protocols.



Zero User Burden: Maintain fluid performance through offline AI self-correction, dynamic resource throttling, and automated battery/RAM optimizers.



2\. Strategic Decisions (Locked; Revisit Only with Explicit Reason)

D1 — Fork RethinkDNS as the IP-layer foundation (see section 1.5 for full multi-channel architecture). Kotlin app + Go/gVisor networking core. Inherit hardened foundation instead of rebuilding. License believed MPL-2.0 — verify in T0.1. We intentionally diverge from upstream's UI going forward (see D10). This fork alone covers IP/TCP/UDP/DNS only — other channels need separate layers.



D2 — Rust for new performance-critical modules, called from Kotlin. Go core stays as-is (full Rust rewrite considered and rejected — see section 12).



D3 — Offline "Fake-Online" works at application level, not raw TLS replay (fresh keys each session defeat replay). Layers: fake network presence → probe responder → fail-fast → junk-domain stubs → on-device Works/Stuck presets → recorded/templated profiles → optional interception where certs aren't pinned.



D4 — Home screen = scrollable resizable tiles, Android-native look and feel (see D10 and section 4.11).



D5 — Build in the cloud. GitHub repo + GitHub Actions builds the APK; no PC required.



D6 — Scanners/auditors scope to networks and devices the user owns or administers (legal boundary, not capability limit).



D7 — Distribution is F-Droid and direct APK, not Google Play (several core features will never pass Play review — expected and fine).



D8 — Piracy-adjacent features permanently out of scope. Protects project's integrity and legal footing.



D9 — Transmission-capable RF features stay passive/detection-only (FCC/wiretap boundary). Detect: yes. Jam or intercept others': no.



D10 — Native Android design language. Material Design components, system font/icon style, standard gestures. App should feel native, never like a custom skin.



D11 — Personal health data permanently out of scope. No tracking, storing, or managing of personal health/medical/biometric data. Device-level health (e.g., battery health F596) and generic "health check" as technical term (e.g., F701, F43) are unaffected.



3\. Non-Negotiable Defaults

Every feature ships off by default

Every server-dependent feature has a documented free, user-controlled path (own server, Tor, public resolver, etc.)

Every sensitive action gets a confirmation step; most sensitive (wipe, lockdown, root toggles) get two-factor confirm (F112)

All user data stays local and encrypted by default

No feature requires an account, login, or phone number to use

Legal Boundary Checklist (section 4.12): Every new feature checked against FCC/CFAA/DMCA/Wiretap/CALEA boundaries

4\. Future-Proofing: Editing, Access, Alteration, and Contribution

4.1 Plugin/Module Architecture — F213

Every feature is an independent module with standard manifest (name, version, permissions, tiles provided, settings schema). Core app is thin shell that loads modules.



4.2 Module Contract (Every F-number, No Exceptions)

Manifest: id, name, version, required permissions, declared tile(s), settings schema

Versioned schemas (4.3): Never raw key-value pairs

Migration path: For every schema version bump

Off by default: Own enable/disable switch, same pattern regardless of complexity

Unit tests: Runnable independently in CI

Doc comment: One paragraph: what it does, permissions needed and why, what breaks if disabled

Input Sandboxing (F327): If module reads externally-sourced or attacker-influenced data (traffic, domain names, packet contents) AND has any ability to act, it follows Input Sandboxing pattern: read access only, data always labeled untrusted content, actions capped by Authority Ladder (section 5.21)

4.3 Versioned Config and Profile Schemas — F214

Every stored structure carries schema version from day one, with migration function for every change.



4.4 Internal API Stability and Documentation — F215

Module interfaces documented and semver'd independently of app's release number; docs auto-generate in CI.



4.5 Contribution Pipeline — F216

All community submissions (blocklists, F07 profiles, F170 VoIP directory, F161 picker entries, F190 rule packs, F243/F317/F319 device compatibility) go through GitHub PR to dedicated data repo, validated automatically by GitHub Action, then merged. Code contributions get human review against Module Contract.



4.6 Feature Flags — F217

Experimental modules ship hidden/beta-gated, enabling small checkpointed development without ever shipping broken default.



4.7 Developer Console — F212

Sandboxed in-app terminal (unlocked like Android's developer options): read/query access to config, logs, modules; scripting hook (Lua/JS) for custom rules, Fake-Online templates, packet-inspector filters; direct hand-editing of F07 profiles; command history, export/import feeding back through F216. Full raw root shell is separate, explicitly-confirmed elevated mode.



4.8 Theming/Localization — F218, F219

F218: Skinning system decoupled from logic. F219: Externalized, translation-ready strings with community translation path via F216.



4.9 Release Channels — F220

Stable/Beta/Nightly, all from same CI pipeline on different branches.



4.10 Local-First Crash/Error Reporting — F221

Opt-in only, user sees full report before ever sent, no silent telemetry.



4.11 Tile Grid and Design System — F95 (Expanded)

Locked spec, based on user-provided screenshot:



Grid: 8-wide logical grid



1x1: Icon + short label (long-press "shrink" option)

2x2: Icon, label, 1-2 stat lines (default, matches screenshot's 2-per-row)

4x2: Full-width short strip (status bar/ticker)

4x4/"tall": Full-width, scrolls own content internally (traffic graphs, scrolling logs)

Long-press enters jiggle/resize mode, drag handle to snap sizes, tap elsewhere to lock in — identical to Android's home-screen widget resize



\+ tile opens searchable, categorized tile gallery



F888 Coming Soon Tiles: Unbuilt features appear in gallery, marked "Coming Soon" with one-line description from Five Lenses' Describe content. Tapping shows what it'll do and why it's waiting.



Root Settings placement: Every category's settings screen ends with "Root Settings" subsection, always visible (never hidden) but grayed out/non-interactive on unrooted device



Settings tile opens category list (Network, Privacy, Firewall, Device, Appearance, Advanced...) exactly like stock Android Settings' drill-down. Every feature's settings live under right category.



Not bound by true Android home-screen widget limits — only ceiling is screen height, handled by internal scrolling



4.12 Legal Boundary Checklist

Every new feature checked against these before added to register:



FCC: No actual RF transmission beyond certified hardware limits; no jamming, ever

CFAA: Scanning/auditing tools default to and gated on user's own network/devices (D6)

DMCA 1201: De-Google/driver-extraction stays in "extract and reuse for interoperability" territory — never defeats Verified Boot's signature chain. This is why Section 11 exists.

Wiretap (ECPA): Mesh/chaff/PTT features handle only user's own traffic; never intercept others' communications

CALEA: Mesh/PTT/anonymization stay device-to-device; no telecom interconnection without separately addressing law

1\. MISSION AND VISION

1.1 Core Mission

Build the most thoughtful, complete, intuitive network-control and privacy app Android has ever had — built from passion and care for the people who'll use it, not to extract money from them. Free. Open source. Powerful enough for experts, simple enough for a grandparent, honest about what it can't do. Everything most apps charge for, we give away; only server-dependent parts need a documented free workaround.



Guiding standard for every decision: Would a product built by someone who actually cared about the user, instead of a deadline and a paycheck, look like this?



1.2 The Sovereign Design Mandate (From Gemini.md – Equal Weight to User Vision)

SERF is designed to grant users total, non-binary, and legally compliant authority over their mobile device hardware, operating system interactions, network streams, home network infrastructure, and application execution environments. It functions as a secure runtime container that intercepts, reconstructs, and sanitizes app behaviors, network requests, and OS interactions without requiring destructive operating system flashing or permanent root access unless explicitly elected.



Five Pillars of Sovereignty:



Distrust Everything: Assume all inbound code, files, network packets, web pages, and third-party binaries are hostile until proven otherwise.

Recheck Everything: Apply real-time telemetry inspection, kernel packet filtering, and local AI behavioral monitoring.

Rebuild Everything: Use AST de-obfuscation, Content Disarm and Reconstruction (CDR), and DOM structural normalizers to synthesize safe, clean execution payloads.

Resend Everything: Route sovereign communications over decentralized peer-to-peer mesh networks, encrypted WebRTC streams, and zero-server signaling protocols.

Zero User Burden: Maintain fluid performance through offline AI self-correction, dynamic resource throttling, and automated battery/RAM optimizers.

1.3 The Full, Explicit Target

No communication reaching or leaving the user's device, across any channel — cellular, Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, NFC, USB, or any other — that the user cannot see, understand in plain language, and choose to block, spoof, alter, or allow. This is not an observability tool. Every piece of information in transit, in either direction, is editable by the user, at minimum for testing purposes.



1.4 Origin Story and Evolution (From CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf)

The project originated from the user's frustration with apps that "require" an internet connection to function, even when offline functionality should be possible. The core insight from the original Claude conversation: "requires internet" means four different things, each with varying difficulty to fake:





Tier



What the App is Actually Doing



Difficulty



Solution



0

Connectivity check, ad/analytics SDK, license ping

Easy

Sinkhole the domain and return a stub 200/204. Often fixes half of all apps.

1

Fetching static config or assets

Easy

Record and replay works perfectly.

2

Login, save sync, entitlement checks

Medium

Responses contain tokens, timestamps, nonces. Pure replay breaks; need templated responses with dynamic field injection.

3

Real server logic (multiplayer, server-side game state, RNG, matchmaking)

Not fixable by replay

Requires a server emulator or alternative architecture. Crowdsourced logs cannot solve this.

Key Architectural Insight from Origin: The Fake-Online approach works because many apps only check "did I get a response?" Old recorded data still satisfies this. However, Tier 3 (real server logic) is fundamentally out of reach for recording/replay approaches.

Every piece of information in transit, in either direction, is editable by the user via:

F664 Live Intercept and Edit

F921 Conditional Injection Rules

F1006 Dynamic Field Auto-Discovery

UWB / LoRa / SDR-received signals

Each requires separate, parallel interception and control layers. The end state is one coherent system where every channel reports through the same unified interface (Transmission Ledger F663 + Dynamic Field Auto-Discovery F1006).

2\. STRATEGIC DECISIONS (Locked; Revisit Only with Explicit Reason)

D1 — Fork RethinkDNS as the IP-layer foundation (see section 1.5 for full multi-channel architecture). Kotlin app + Go/gVisor networking core. Inherit a hardened foundation instead of rebuilding it. License believed MPL-2.0 — verify in T0.3. Intentionally diverge from upstream's UI (see D10); our own home screen is permanent. D2 — Rust for new performance-critical modules, called from Kotlin. Go core stays as-is (full Rust rewrite considered and rejected — see section 13 decision log). Rust/Python Split Architecture confirmed (from GEMINIchatFULL.html): Rust handles raw TCP/UDP/IP packet parsing, connection tracking, real-time stream interception, and loopback simulation; Python manages user configuration, rule orchestration, REST/GraphQL control APIs, and automated workflows.



D3 — Offline "Fake-Online" works at the application level, not raw TLS replay (fresh keys per session defeat replay). Layered approach from CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf:

D8 — Piracy-adjacent features permanently out of scope. Protects project integrity and legal footing. Explicitly includes: License/purchase-check removal on others' apps, ad-removal inside paid apps to avoid paying developers, generating fake receipts, patching signed APKs to disable checks. Legitimate alternatives included: System-wide ad/tracker blocking at network level (F10, F1202), per-app firewall (F12), permission control (F100).



D9 — Transmission-capable RF features stay passive/detection-only (FCC/wiretap boundary). Detect: yes. Jam or intercept others': no.



D10 — Native Android design language. Material Design components, system font/icon style, standard gestures (long-press to edit, drag to reorder). Applies to every UI feature by default.



D11 — Personal health data permanently out of scope. No feature tracks, stores, or manages personal health/medical/biometric data. Previously-registered features in this category are removed and marked "(deleted)".



3\. NON-NEGOTIABLE DEFAULTS

Every feature ships off by default.

Every server-dependent feature has a documented free, user-controlled path (own server, Tor, public resolver, etc.).

Every sensitive action gets a confirmation step; the most sensitive (wipe, lockdown, root toggles) get two-factor confirm (F112).

4\. FUTURE-PROOFING: EDITING, ACCESS, ALTERATION, AND CONTRIBUTION

4.1 Plugin/Module Architecture — F213

Every feature is an independent module with a standard manifest (name, version, permissions, tiles provided, settings schema). Core app is a thin shell that loads modules.



From GEMINI.md: Dynamic Plugin Container \& Runtime Hook Engine allows third-party apps, reverse-engineered utility modules, and legacy applications to run inside SERF as integrated modules (.serf\_mod). Runtime interception hooks route network calls through SERF's local VPN/eBPF firewall (F10), storage requests redirect to encrypted .age vaults (F73), and UI components render inside SERF's Tile Grid. Sandboxed memory isolation prevents crashing modules from affecting the host system. One-File Automated Migration (.serf\_backup) serializes all modules, terminal scripts, firewall policies, and layout configurations into a single AES-256 encrypted file.



4.2 Module Contract (Every F-number, No Exceptions)

Manifest: id, name, version, required permissions, declared tile(s), settings schema.

Settings in a versioned schema (4.3), never raw key-value pairs.

Migration path for every schema version bump.

Off by default, own enable/disable switch, same pattern regardless of complexity.

Unit tests runnable independently in CI.

One-paragraph doc comment: What it does, what permissions it needs and why, what breaks if disabled.

Input Sandboxing Pattern (F327): If the module reads externally-sourced or attacker-influenced data (traffic, domain names, packet contents) and has any ability to act, it follows: read access only, data always labeled untrusted content, actions capped by the Authority Ladder (section 5.21).

Expert Custom Thresholds (F875): Via F212 Developer Console for advanced configuration.

From GEMINI.md: The Authority Ladder governing AI and autonomous actions:



Observe \& explain (always on)

Suggest (default — shows recommendation, waits for a tap)

Act with one-tap confirm (medium-stakes: block a domain, enable a rule)

Act autonomously (only fully reversible, low-stakes, user-whitelisted actions)

Never autonomous: Wipe, lockdown, root toggles, vault access, anything on the F112 two-factor list. The assistant may recommend; it may never execute.

4.3 Versioned Config and Profile Schemas — F214

Every stored structure carries a schema version from day one, with a migration function for every change.

Module interfaces are documented and semver'd independently of the app's release number; docs auto-generate in CI.

Code contributions get human review against the Module Contract.

Experimental modules ship hidden/beta-gated, enabling small checkpointed development without ever shipping a broken default.

FCC: No actual RF transmission beyond certified hardware limits; no jamming, ever, in any form.

CFAA: Scanning/auditing tools default to and are gated on the user's own network/devices (D6).

DMCA 1201: De-Google/driver-extraction tooling stays in "extract and reuse for interoperability" territory (LineageOS legal basis) — never defeats Verified Boot's signature chain. This boundary is the entire reason section 11 (Parking Lot) exists.

Wiretap (ECPA): Mesh/chaff/PTT features handle only the user's own traffic; never intercept others' communications without consent.

CALEA/telecom law: Mesh/PTT/anonymization features stay device-to-device; no real telecom interconnection without separately addressing this body of law.

Carrier/Telecom Separation (from GEMINI.md): Spoofing or forging subscriber identities (IMSI, IMEI, cryptographic SIM keys, cell tower handshakes) to deceive telecommunication networks or obtain unbilled service is strictly illegal and hard-locked out of SERF's RF engine. However, spoofing these exact data points locally to third-party apps (e.g., feeding a fake Android ID to a tracking app) is 100% legal privacy sandboxing and fully supported.

No Binary Tampering (from GEMINI.md): Never decompile, alter, re-sign, or patch third-party application binaries (.apk/.dex/.smali). All runtime modifications occur via safe memory hooks, local network proxies, or standard Android package management APIs (Shizuku, ADB, PackageManager).

4.13 AI Assistant Input Sandboxing — F327

The on-device/optional-cloud assistant (F323–F327, section 5.21) only ever gets read access to traffic data, always labeled as untrusted content, never as instructions. Its actions are capped strictly by the Authority Ladder regardless of what it reads, specifically because traffic data (domain names, packet contents) is attacker-influenced and a known prompt-injection surface.



From GEMINI.md: The Self-Healing AI Telemetry \& Anti-Exploit Sanitization Sandbox (F1200) provides continuous local AI monitoring tracking CPU, RAM, and battery impact across all rebuilding modules. Automatically throttles heavy sanitization profiles on lagging domains and surfaces optimization guidance via the Suggest lens. Enforces strict zero-trust isolation (ephemeral child sandboxes, hard execution timeouts, memory limits) for all AST de-obfuscation and CDR tasks.



4.14 The Five Lenses Standard — F404 (Module Contract 4.2.8)

Every feature, no exceptions, is built with five consistent lenses:



Monitor (live status on the tile)

Detect (flags something notable now)

Explain (plain-English "what is this," reusing F181's pattern everywhere)

Describe (a permanent reference doc, one tap away)

Suggest (a recommendation, never auto-applied — Suggest is always Authority Ladder step 2)

Four additional lenses apply where relevant:



Compare (F405, vs. last week/last version, ties to F228)

Simulate (F406, dry-run a change before applying)

History/Timeline (F407, scrollable record of what a feature has done)

Export (F408, every feature's data leaves in open CSV/JSON, never trapped)

From GEMINI.md: Every tile across the SERF dashboard acts as an interactive portal. Tapping any tile opens its dedicated configuration hub equipped with the standardized Five Lenses Navigation Suite.



4.15 No Hidden Defaults — F423 (Module Contract 4.2.9)

Any underlying setting Android, a library, or RethinkDNS's own core exposes — even ones buried in code with no UI — gets a corresponding visible toggle in SERF's settings hierarchy. Before a feature is "done," every meaningful internal tunable must appear in its settings screen under the right F95 category.



4.16 Resource Monitor \& Live Status — F424–F426 (Module Contract 4.2.10)

Every feature wires into a central Resource Monitor Tile (F424) showing live battery/data/CPU cost per feature. Every feature with an on/off state carries a Universal Live Status Indicator (F425: green/active, amber/degraded, red/failed) visible on its tile and settings screen at all times, plus an "Is It Actually Working" Self-Test (F426) that actively verifies state rather than just reporting what it assumes.



4.17 True Build-Time Removability — F438 (Module Contract 4.2.11)

Every module lives in its own package, reachable only through the Module SDK's defined interface (F213/F215) — never directly into another module's internals. A build manifest lets anyone check/uncheck which F-numbers compile into a given build. Cross-module calls through anything other than the defined SDK interface are a build error, not a style violation.



4.18 Search-First Component Reuse — F584 (Module Contract 4.2.16)

Before any module is built, search for an existing open-source project that already does it, or most of it — check section 4.18's Reusable Components Registry first, search GitHub/PyPI/F-Droid if not listed, and only build from scratch if nothing suitable exists, the license doesn't fit, or the project is abandoned.

4.20 Mandatory Pre-Build Research Step — Dual-Direction, Every Time (Module Contract 4.2.18)

Before writing a single line of code, config, or UI for ANY part of the app — new F-number, previously-planned one, or revisiting/upgrading something already built — the first action, every time, with no exceptions, is TWO searches:



Advancement Search: Current advancements, standards, patches, better algorithms, and open-source repos relevant to that specific part. Even features planned long ago — a feature's original spec is a snapshot, not a ceiling.

Weakness Search (equally mandatory, never skipped): Current known bugs, vulnerabilities, exploits, attack patterns, failure modes, outdated/unpatched components, deprecated libraries, and missing hardening relevant to that specific part — and to whatever approach, library, or technique #1 is about to adopt.

Only after both searches return (or confirm nothing relevant exists) does actual building begin. This is not the same as checking the Reusable Components Registry (4.18) — it's a live, current check every time.



Goal stated plainly: The most hardened app possible for today's technology and threat landscape, not just the most capable one.

5.0c Original Fake-Online Architecture (From CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf — Foundational Concepts)

Proposed Architecture from Origin:



Steering Layer: Android VpnService (or DNS rule set like Rethink's) that redirects target app's domains to a local server on the device.

Local Mock Server: Reads an app profile (JSON) with rules like "if request matches X, respond with Y." Matching uses method, path, and selected body fields. Responses support templating ({{now}}, {{uuid}}, {{echo request.field}}).

Recorder Mode: Same app, in capture mode, logs request/response pairs, strips secrets, exports a draft profile.

Community Profiles: Keyed by package name and version, with "works / partially works / broken" rating.

Suggested Path from Origin:



Prototype by hand on one app with PCAPdroid or mitmproxy, sort traffic into tiers.

Define profile format (HAR-like JSON + rules + templating layer).

Build mock server and steering app.

Add recorder and sanitizer, then community database.

From CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf — The Simplest Version That Works (MVP):



VpnService that captures all traffic (or only chosen apps')

Tiny HTTP responder for connectivity probes

Per-app toggle for "return an instant fake failure for everything else"

This alone fixes the "requires internet just to launch" apps — the largest group.

Automated routine: Force-stop and disable Google Play Services → launch game → re-enable Play Services automatically once game is up (or when closed).

Multi-Hop Country Chain Routing (F30): Empowers users to select sequenced string of countries via map/list. Rust engine dynamically compiles chained WireGuard/SOCKS5 routing path.

Zero-Knowledge Encrypted Egress Chaining (F31): Route specific app traffic through custom SOCKS5, Tor, or multi-hop WireGuard tunnels on per-app basis.

System-Wide Stealth Mode (F13): Strict default-drop policy on unsolicited inbound packets. Device invisible to port scans.

Advanced Packet Capture (F40): Raw HTTP/HTTPS header views, binary payload inspection, .pcap exports from UI.

Zero-Trust Rogue Network Protection (F36): Cross-references public Wi-Fi BSSIDs and SSL cert chains, isolates unencrypted traffic or forces WireGuard tunnel.

From GEMINI.md:



F1190 Granular Content \& Element Control HUD: Per-site/global toggle for JavaScript, audio/video, web fonts, WebGL, iFrames, analytics beacons. "Load Everything" master preset.

9\. BUILD ORDER — Difficulty-and-Priority Tiers, Server-Dependency Ordered (Reorganized)

9.0 Server-Dependency Ordering Principle (Applied as Additional, Higher-Weight Sort Axis)

Four real levels, ordered No-to-Yes:



(1) No dependency — fully on-device, works the instant the app installs.

(2) Public free infrastructure, zero setup by us — the Tor network, public DNS resolvers, public CT logs, GitHub itself (already hosting everything for free).

(3) Self-hosted, even if free — a coordination server (Headscale), a relay (OvenMediaEngine), something someone has to stand up and keep running.

(4) Costs money — a required paid service.

Confirmed: Nothing in this register was ever designed to require Level 4 (section 3's defaults already mandate a free path for every server-dependent feature), so Level 4 should end up essentially empty — that's the correct outcome, not a gap to fill.



Level 3 items are deliberately pushed to the end of the build order below, regardless of how early their Priority/Difficulty alone would otherwise place them, so a working, feature-rich app ships well before any self-hosted infrastructure is needed.



Reorganization scope, stated honestly: The Feature Register (section 5) already groups all 1000+ features into 38 relevant thematic sections (5.0–5.38) — that grouping stays as-is; re-sorting it feature-by-feature into new categories would risk losing content for no real gain. What's rebuilt here is the order of construction: old alphabetical Phases A–R are replaced with tiers ordered by actual priority-then-difficulty, using each feature's existing Pri and Diff ratings (section 5's tables).



Within any tier, build the Diff-1/2 items in that tier's range before the Diff-4/5 items — the Diff column already in each table is the tiebreaker.



Why the hardest tier never requires rewriting earlier work (structural guarantee): This follows directly from F213 (Module SDK) and F438 (build-time removability) — every feature is an independent module reachable only through the defined SDK interface (Module Contract 4.2.11). A Tier 9 module can depend on a Tier 1 module's interface, but nothing in an earlier tier is ever modified to accommodate a later one. If this guarantee is ever at risk for a specific feature, that's exactly what Fork Decision Protocol (4.19) exists to catch before building, not after.



Section 11 (Parking Lot) stays excluded from every tier until explicitly un-parked.



Research-sweep upgrade findings (F688–F830, section 5.36a–ab) are not separate build steps — each applies within its parent feature's own tier, per the "Upgrades" column in those tables.
