1\. EXECUTIVE INTENT \& CORE PHILOSOPHY

1.1 Project Intent (Synthesized from All Sources)

SERF (Sovereign Environment \& Runtime Framework) is designed to grant users total, non-binary, and legally compliant authority over their mobile device hardware, operating system interactions, network streams, home network infrastructure, and application execution environments. It functions as a secure runtime container that intercepts, reconstructs, and sanitizes app behaviors, network requests, and OS interactions without requiring destructive operating system flashing or permanent root access unless explicitly elected.



Core Directive: Compile and build the ultimate all-in-one, user-sovereign, unassailable Android security container that completely replaces reliance on Google-centric services, tracking vectors, and restrictive telecom boundaries while maintaining absolute structural hygiene, zero-trust sanitization, anti-forensic lockdown, and lightning-fast fluid performance.



1.2 The Sovereign Design Mandate (Gemini.md + CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf)

Distrust Everything: Assume all inbound code, files, network packets, web pages, and third-party binaries are hostile until proven otherwise.

Recheck Everything: Apply real-time telemetry inspection, kernel packet filtering, and local AI behavioral monitoring.

Rebuild Everything: Use AST de-obfuscation, Content Disarm and Reconstruction (CDR), and DOM structural normalizers to synthesize safe, clean execution payloads.

Resend Everything: Route sovereign communications over decentralized peer-to-peer mesh networks, encrypted WebRTC streams, and zero-server signaling protocols.

Zero User Burden: Maintain fluid performance through offline AI self-correction, dynamic resource throttling, and automated battery/RAM optimizers.

1.3 Mission and Vision (State.md - Expanded)

Build the most thoughtful, complete, intuitive network-control and privacy app Android has ever had — built from passion and care for the people who'll use it, not to extract money from them. Free. Open source. Powerful enough for experts, simple enough for a grandparent, honest about what it can't do.



The full, explicit target: No communication reaching or leaving the user's device, across any channel — cellular, Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, NFC, USB, or any other — that the user cannot see, understand in plain language, and choose to block, spoof, alter, or allow. This is not an observability tool. Every piece of information in transit, in either direction, is editable by the user, at minimum for testing purposes.



Guiding standard for every decision: Would a product built by someone who actually cared about the user, instead of a deadline and a paycheck, look like this?



1.4 The Real-World Context (CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf)

Most products and services are built for revenue, not user benefit. They are thin, bare-minimum, thoughtless, careless, and uncreative. SERF is different: built from passion, not money. It incorporates immense functionality while remaining simplistic and intuitive. It covers every need with thought and care.



Why this matters: Millions of people will be glad there's someone who actually cares more about people than money — who is thoughtful, willing, patient, and builds a product people love.



2\. LEGAL BOUNDARIES \& COMPLIANCE FRAMEWORK

2.1 Non-Negotiable Legal Constraints (State.md D8, D9, D11 + Gemini.md + CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf)

D8 - Piracy-Adjacent Features Permanently Out of Scope:



Never decompile, alter, re-sign, or patch third-party application binaries (.apk/.dex/.smali).

All runtime modifications occur via safe memory hooks, local network proxies, or standard Android package management APIs (Shizuku, ADB, PackageManager).

No commercial license theft: Do not bypass commercial DRM or crack proprietary cloud-based in-app purchases.

Billing/license test utilities operate strictly within local sandboxed loopback environments.

D9 - Transmission-Capable RF Features Stay Passive/Detection-Only:



FCC Compliant: RFID/NFC obfuscation uses standard low-power Host Card Emulation (HCE) protocols.

No active RF jamming across regulated spectrums — strictly prohibited.

Detect: Yes. Jam or intercept others': No.

D11 - Personal Health Data Permanently Out of Scope:



No feature tracks, stores, or manages personal health/medical/biometric data.

Examples of what this excludes: period/menstrual tracking, medication reminders, neural/brain data.

Device-level health (e.g., battery health, F596) and generic "health check" as a technical/network term are unaffected.

Additional Boundaries from CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf:



Environmental Jurisdiction: Honor the user's ownership of physical hardware. Enforce control at the hardware boundary (socket tagging, local VPN filtering, memory heap clearing, HAL sensor mocking).

Strict Carrier/Telecom Separation: Spoofing or forging subscriber identities (IMSI, IMEI, cryptographic SIM keys, cell tower handshakes) to deceive telecommunication networks or obtain unbilled service is strictly illegal and hard-locked out of SERF's RF engine.

However: Spoofing these exact data points locally to third-party apps (e.g., feeding a fake Android ID to a tracking app) is 100% legal privacy sandboxing and fully supported.

2.2 Legal Boundary Checklist (State.md 4.12 - Expanded)

Every new feature gets checked against these before being added to the register:





Boundary



Constraint



Status



FCC

No actual RF transmission beyond certified hardware limits; no jamming, ever

Hard Lock

CFAA

Scanning/auditing tools default to and are gated on user's own network/devices

Enforced

DMCA 1201

De-Google/driver-extraction tooling stays in "extract and reuse for interoperability" territory (LineageOS legal basis) — never defeats Verified Boot's signature chain

Enforced

Wiretap (ECPA)

Mesh/chaff/PTT features handle only user's own traffic; never intercept others' communications without consent

Enforced

CALEA/Telecom Law

Mesh/PTT/anonymization features stay device-to-device; no real telecom interconnection without separately addressing law

Enforced

SMS Verification Bypass

Cannot and will not build tools to spoof SMS verification or bypass carrier checks

Hard Lock

Account Takeover

No tools for intercepting someone else's SMS or defeating server-side verification

Hard Lock

Parking Lot Note: The entire reason Section 11 (Parking Lot) exists is to preserve legally ambiguous features for future reconsideration when the legal landscape changes, without ever implementing them prematurely.



3\. STRATEGIC DECISIONS (Locked; Revisit Only with Explicit Reason)

3.1 Core Foundation Decisions (State.md D1-D11 + CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf)

D1 - Fork RethinkDNS as the IP-Layer Foundation:



What: Kotlin app + Go/gVisor networking core. Inherit a hardened foundation instead of rebuilding.

License: Believed MPL-2.0 — verify in T0.3.

UI Divergence: Intentionally diverge from upstream's UI. Our own home screen is permanent design.

Scope: This fork covers IP/TCP/UDP/DNS traffic only — Bluetooth, NFC, cellular signaling, and USB each need separate layers (see Section 1.5).

Rationale from CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf: Forking RethinkDNS beats a rewrite. Its Go core already handles the hard packet work (user-space TCP stack). A full Rust rewrite would cost months. Python on Android is awkward (requires bundling interpreter, adds size, startup/memory costs, speed issues).

D2 - Rust for New Performance-Critical Modules:



Called from Kotlin. Go core stays as-is.

Full Rust rewrite considered and rejected (see State.md section 12 decision log).

Rationale: Rust wins on no garbage collector, memory safety, smaller footprint. But Go's gVisor-based network stack is battle-tested. Rewriting would reintroduce bugs already fixed. Upstream updates would be lost. Gain is modest — Go is already fast enough.

D3 - Offline "Fake-Online" Works at Application Level:



Not raw TLS replay (fresh keys each session defeat replay).

Layers: Fake network presence → probe responder → fail-fast → junk-domain stubs → on-device Works/Stuck presets → recorded/templated profiles → optional interception where certs aren't pinned.

Origin: Directly from CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf's "Google Play Sucks" concept.

D4 - Home Screen = Scrollable Resizable Tiles:



Android-native look and feel (see D10 and Section 4.11 for full grid spec).

D5 - Build in the Cloud:



GitHub repo + GitHub Actions builds the APK; no PC required.

D6 - Scanners/Auditors Scope to Networks and Devices User Owns/Administers:



Legal boundary, not a capability limit.

D7 - Distribution is F-Droid and Direct APK:



Not Google Play (several core features will never pass Play review — expected and fine).

D10 - Native Android Design Language:



Material Design components, system font/icon style, standard gestures (long-press to edit, drag to reorder).

3.2 Additional Decisions from CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf

D12 - App Name Evolution:



Originally "Google Play Sucks" → Now SERF (Sovereign Environment \& Runtime Framework).

D13 - Python's Role:



Not on the phone: Bundling Python interpreter adds tens of MB, startup/memory costs, speed issues, library trouble.

Valid uses: Build/test scripts in cloud, blocklist/profile tools, analysis scripts for recorded traffic on a computer.

If needed: Can add via Chaquopy, but not as a foundation.

D14 - Scope Philosophy:



Everything gets incorporated. No ideas are dropped. The only decision is order of implementation.

Sixty features is not a plan. On free tier, working from a phone, that's years of work. Most features go into backlog, with only the offline engine as core initially.

4\. NON-NEGOTIABLE DEFAULTS

Every feature ships off by default.

Every server-dependent feature has a documented free, user-controlled path (own server, Tor, public resolver, etc.).

Every sensitive action gets a confirmation step; most sensitive (wipe, lockdown, root toggles) get two-factor confirm (F112).

All user data stays local and encrypted by default. Sync/backup is user-initiated, to storage the user controls.

No feature requires an account, login, or phone number to use.

Tile system rule: Switching a tile off switches off the code behind it (battery/memory efficiency).

5\. FUTURE-PROOFING: EDITING, ACCESS, ALTERATION, AND CONTRIBUTION

5.1 Plugin/Module Architecture — F213

Every feature is an independent module with a standard manifest (name, version, permissions, tiles provided, settings schema). Core app is a thin shell that loads modules.



5.2 Module Contract (Every F-Number, No Exceptions)

Manifest: id, name, version, required permissions, declared tile(s), settings schema.

Settings: Versioned schema (5.3), never raw key-value pairs.

Migration: Path for every schema version bump.

Default State: Off by default, own enable/disable switch, same pattern regardless of complexity.

Testing: Unit tests runnable independently in CI.

Documentation: One-paragraph doc comment: what it does, what permissions it needs and why, what breaks if disabled.

Input Sandboxing: If module reads externally-sourced or attacker-influenced data (traffic, domain names, packet contents) and has any ability to act, it follows the pattern established by F327: read access only, data always labeled untrusted content, actions capped by the Authority Ladder (Section 5.21), regardless of what the data says.

5.3 Versioned Config and Profile Schemas — F214

Every stored structure carries a schema version from day one, with a migration function for every change.



5.4 Internal API Stability and Documentation — F215

Module interfaces are documented and semver'd independently of the app's release number; docs auto-generate in CI.



5.5 Contribution Pipeline — F216

All community submissions (blocklists, F07 profiles, F170 VoIP directory, F161 picker entries, F190 rule packs, F243/F317/F319 device compatibility and patch data) go through:



GitHub PR to dedicated data repo

Validated automatically by GitHub Action

Then merged

Code contributions get human review against Module Contract

5.6 Feature Flags — F217

Experimental modules ship hidden/beta-gated, enabling small checkpointed development without ever shipping a broken default.



5.7 Developer Console — F212

Sandboxed in-app terminal (unlocked like Android's own developer options):



Read/query access to config, logs, modules

Scripting hook (Lua/JS) for custom rules, Fake-Online templates, packet-inspector filters

Direct hand-editing of F07 profiles

Command history, export/import feeding back through F216

Full raw root shell is a separate, explicitly-confirmed elevated mode, not the default

5.8 Theming/Localization — F218, F219

F218: Skinning system decoupled from logic.

F219: Externalized, translation-ready strings with community translation path via F216.

5.9 Release Channels — F220

Stable/Beta/Nightly, all from the same CI pipeline on different branches.



5.10 Local-First Crash/Error Reporting — F221

Opt-in only. User sees the full report before it's ever sent. No silent telemetry.



5.11 AI Assistant Input Sandboxing — F327

The on-device/optional-cloud assistant (F323–F327, Section 5.21) only ever gets read access to traffic data, always labeled as untrusted content, never as instructions. Its actions are capped strictly by the Authority Ladder regardless of what it reads, specifically because traffic data (domain names, packet contents) is attacker-influenced and a known prompt-injection surface.



5.12 The Five Lenses Standard — F404

Every feature, no exceptions, is built with five consistent lenses:



Monitor (live status on the tile)

Detect (flags something notable now)

Explain (plain-English "what is this," reusing F181's pattern everywhere)

Describe (a permanent reference doc, one tap away)

Suggest (a recommendation, never auto-applied — Suggest)

5.13 Authority Ladder (Referenced by Module Contract 4.2.7)

Defines what the system can do based on user intent, never exceeding the user's explicit authority. Caps AI assistant actions regardless of input.



6\. CORE ARCHITECTURE (Synthesized from All Sources)

6.1 Architectural Overview (Gemini.md + CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf)

SERF implements a multi-tiered execution model that dynamically selects the highest available privilege layer without requiring permanent root if non-root alternatives exist.



text

Copy

+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

|                            SERF USER INTERFACE (UI)                               |

|          Tile Grid System, Widget Studio \& Settings Drill-Down Architecture       |

+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

&#x20;                                        │

+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

|                LOCAL SERVICE, CONTAINER \& INTERCEPTION CORE                       |

+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

&#x20; │                  │                             │                           │

&#x20; ▼                  ▼                             ▼                           ▼

\[eBPF / Traffic]  \[RethinkDNS Local VPN]   \[Shizuku / ADB IPC]      \[Sensor HAL / HCE]

&#x20; │                  │                             │                           │

&#x20; ▼                  ▼                             ▼                           ▼

&#x20;Kernel Sockets   Packet \& DNS Firewall   Package/Component Manager  Hardware \& Radio Subsystem

6.2 Layer Specifications

1\. eBPF (Extended Berkeley Packet Filter):



Injected directly into the Android kernel network layer to tag, inspect, and filter network socket events (sys\_enter\_connect, sys\_enter\_sendto) with minimal CPU overhead.

Purpose: Deep packet inspection at kernel level.

2\. Local VpnService Loopback Stack (Forked RethinkDNS Core):



Intercepts device-wide TCP/UDP network streams.

Performs local DNS-over-HTTPS (DoH) resolution, SNI inspection, and port-level rule enforcement completely on-device.

Key Insight from CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf: RethinkDNS's Go core is built on gVisor's network stack — battle-tested by Google.

3\. Shizuku / ADB IPC Framework:



Utilizes system-level Android Debug Bridge permissions to execute package management commands (pm disable-user, appops set), inspect system logs (logcat), and manage hidden system service flags without modifying /system or /vendor.

Purpose: No-root access to system-level controls.

4\. Host Card Emulation (HCE) \& HAL Hooks:



Interfaces directly with NFC radios and sensor subroutines to manage physical privacy, RFID obfuscation, and synthetic sensor data injection.

6.3 Offline Engine Architecture (CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf - Core Concept)

The "Fake-Online" Simulator Engine:



Record Mode: Tag an app, flip on Live Recording. The Rust engine hooks into the app's outbound socket streams, logging bidirectional payloads, packet timestamps, TLS handshake patterns, and destination IPs/domains.

Mock State: Maps out regular keep-alives, handshakes, and response signatures.

Playback / Offline Mode: When app is booted offline, intercepts local socket/DNS calls, spins up local loopback mock responder, plays back recorded request-response cycles precisely as they occurred live. App's network stack believes it's talking to remote servers.

Tiered Approach (From CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf):





Tier



What the App is Doing



Difficulty



Solution



0

Connectivity check, ad/analytics SDK, license ping

Easy

Sinkhole domain, return stub 200/204. Fixes \~50% of apps.

1

Fetching static config or assets

Easy

Record and replay works.

2

Login, save sync, entitlement checks

Medium

Pure replay breaks (tokens, timestamps, nonces). Need templated responses.

3

Real server logic (multiplayer, server-side state, RNG)

Not Fixable

Requires server emulator. Out of scope for replay.

Crowdsourced logs work for Tiers 0-2. Tier 3 requires server emulation.



6.4 Self-Learning Approach (CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf - Efficient Version)

Instead of requiring full playthrough recordings:



1\. Fake Network Presence (Universal, No Per-App Data):



VpnService creates virtual network that Android treats as online.

Built-in responder answers connectivity probe (generate\_204).

2\. Built-in Knowledge of Junk Traffic (Universal, Shipped Once):



Most background traffic is analytics, ads, crash reporting, attribution SDKs (Firebase, Crashlytics, AdMob, AppsFlyer, Adjust, Unity, Facebook).

One bundled domain list (maintained like ad-block list) gets instant stub answers (204, or 200 with empty body).

3\. Fail Fast on Everything Else:



For unknown domains, return immediate clean error (connection refused or 503).

Apps with offline fallback handle quick error better than hang or fake 200 with garbage.

4\. Learn on Device, Not from Recordings:



For stuck apps, tool tries strategies per domain:

Fast error, empty 200, {}, \[], 204, delayed timeout

User taps "Works" or "Stuck"

Tool moves to next preset, remembers what worked

Few taps instead of full playthrough.

5\. Read the APK Instead of Watching It:



Static analysis extracts hostnames, URL strings, Firebase config, network security config before running app.

Pre-sorts most of step 4.

6\. Share Tiny Results, Not Logs:



Community database stores: "App X v1.4: domains A and B on preset 2, domain C on fast-fail = works."

Few bytes, no personal data, no recording needed.

7\. Optional Root/LSPosed Module:



Only for apps that read airplane mode or SIM state directly.

Spoofs those API results.

Most users won't need it.

6.5 Hybrid Rust + Python Split Architecture (GEMINIchatFULL.html)

The Rust Engine (The Data Plane):



Built around cross-platform TUN/TAP virtual network interface or local proxy stack.

Handles raw TCP/UDP/IP packet parsing, connection tracking (conntrack), real-time stream interception, and loopback simulation with near-zero memory overhead and maximum throughput.

The Python Layer (The Control Plane):



Manages user rules, handles configuration files, integrates blocklist downloads (like uBlock Origin lists).

Exposes REST/GraphQL control API.

Orchestrates complex automation workflows (recording schedules, playback triggers).

Note: Python on Android requires Chaquopy or similar, adding tens of MB to APK, startup/memory costs. Recommended: Use Python for cloud-based tools (build scripts, blocklist conversion, traffic analysis) but not as phone foundation. Kotlin handles control plane on Android.



6.6 Shared Engines (Architectural Note - State.md 8)

AP Engine: Underlies F287–F296, F338–F340. One shared hotspot-creation/management module.

Mesh Comms Engine: Underlies F91, F107, F134, F292–F309. One shared relay/voice/text transport.

Shizuku/ADB Layer (F321): Underlies F96, F100, F310, and any future no-root feature needing shell-level access.

7\. TOTAL COMMUNICATION COVERAGE — THE ACTUAL GOAL

7.1 Explicit Goal Statement (State.md 1.5)

By the end of the build, there is no communication reaching or leaving the user's device, across any channel, that the user cannot see, understand in plain language, and choose to block, spoof, alter, or allow — not just observe.



This is not a monitoring app. Every piece of information in transit, in either direction, is editable by the user, at minimum for testing purposes.



7.2 Honest Architectural Reality (State.md 1.5)

RethinkDNS's forked core (D1) provides deep, genuine coverage of ONE layer — IP-layer traffic over Wi-Fi and cellular data (TCP, UDP, DNS, the large majority of everyday communication).



It does NOT cover:



Bluetooth Classic + BLE

NFC

Cellular voice/SMS signaling (circuit-switched domain)

USB data transfer

None of these pass through Android's VPN tunnel. Each is a separate OS subsystem with its own APIs.



7.3 Required Architecture (State.md 1.5 Table)



Channel



Layer Needed



Already Specified At



Status



IP/TCP/UDP/DNS (Wi-Fi + cellular data)

RethinkDNS fork core (VpnService-based)

D1, D2, Tier 2-3

Core Foundation

Bluetooth Classic + BLE

Android Bluetooth stack hooks + root-tier interception

F132, F134, F905-pattern, F997

Separate Layer

NFC

NFC reader/emulation mode hooks

F117, F1003

Separate Layer

Cellular voice/SMS (circuit-switched)

RIL-level read-only logging (NOT full control)

F975, F976

Read-Only

USB (data transfer)

USB device-mode interception

F260, F382, F1004

Separate Layer

Infrared

IR blaster hooks

F116

Separate Layer

Satellite

Satellite modem interface

F144

Separate Layer

UWB / LoRa / SDR

Dedicated radio hooks, dongle-dependent

F146, F142, F120

Separate Layer

What "rebuild RethinkDNS's core" concretely means:



Full packet-level read AND write access for every flow, not just block/allow decisions (base needed by F662/F664/F921's editing capability).

Module SDK's hook points (F213) so each additional channel layer plugs into the same unified Transmission Ledger (F663) and Dynamic Field Auto-Discovery engine (F1006).

Security hardening practice (F883-887, starting at T0.8) applied to every layer, not just the original IP core.

End state: One coherent system where every channel reports through the same interface, regardless of underlying subsystem.



For any AI or contributor: If a channel isn't in the table above, it isn't covered yet, and that's a real gap to flag, not an oversight to assume is handled.



8\. UI/UX PARADIGM \& WIDGET CUSTOMIZATION STUDIO

8.1 The Universal Five Lenses Standard — F404

Every tile across the SERF dashboard acts as an interactive portal. Tapping any tile opens its dedicated configuration hub equipped with the standardized Five Lenses Navigation Suite:



Monitor (Live Telemetry Tab): Real-time status, network throughput, active socket connections, memory usage, live event logs.

Detect (Anomaly \& Risk Flagging Tab): Active security flags, unrequested background calls, tracking attempts, open vulnerabilities.

Explain (Plain-Language Context Tab): Clear, non-technical descriptions answering what the feature is, why it matters, and its battery/privacy impact.

Describe (Technical Documentation Tab): Exhaustive reference manuals detailing underlying API calls, kernel hooks, socket flags, RFC standards, and hardware registers.

Suggest (Actionable Authority Tab): One-tap, user-approved recommendations complying strictly with the Authority Ladder — SERF suggests optimal rules, but never executes silent, non-consensual system changes.

8.2 Settings Category Index \& One-Tap Tile Creation (Gemini.md 4.2 + State.md 4.11)

SERF mirrors stock Android's hierarchical Settings UI layout. Navigating to Settings surfaces a comprehensive, structured list of all core functional domains.



Core Navigation Categories: Apps (General Settings), Force Stop Apps, Fill \& Clear RAM, Fast Task Killer, Password Manager, Contacts Manager, Keyboard, Sovereign VoIP \& Telephony, Volume Booster, Feature Request Hub, Dimmer (Darker Screen Control), Gallery, Android Eraser, Aurora Store, File Explorer, Radio \& Bluetooth Bridge, Notepad, Background Video Recorder, Advanced Package Installer/Scrubber, Terminal, Root Control, Calculator, Custom One-Click Script Execution Tiles, VPN, Firewall, Bluetooth, NFC, Device Control, Network, Resource Monitor, Logs \& Stats, Process Monitor, Settings, Clock, Tiles \& Widgets Studio, Mesh Network, Proxy, Privacy, Wi-Fi, Notifications, Security, Location \& GPS, Spoofing, Blocking, Smart Assist AI, System, About, Display, Translator, Local Device Server (with My Website), Community Forum, DNS, Other Wireless Connections, SIM \& Mobile, Weather.



Individual \[Tile] Toggles: Next to every category and sub-feature in the Settings menu is an off/on toggle labeled "Tile". Switching this toggle to ON instantly creates a live, interactive tile on the user's main home screen dashboard.



8.3 Tile Grid and Design System — F95 (Expanded from State.md 4.11)

Locked spec, based on user-provided screenshot:



Grid: 8-wide logical grid.

Sizes:

1x1 (icon + short label, the long-press "shrink" option)

2x2 (icon, label, 1–2 stat lines — the default, matches screenshot's 2-per-row layout)

4x2 (full-width short strip, status bar/ticker)

4x4/"tall" (full-width, scrolls its own content internally for live data — traffic graphs, scrolling logs)

Long-press enters jiggle/resize mode, drag a handle to snap sizes, tap elsewhere to lock in — identical to Android's home-screen widget resize.

The + tile (present in reference screenshot) opens a searchable, categorized tile gallery.

F888 Coming Soon Tiles: Unbuilt features (especially Tier 11's self-hosted-infrastructure-dependent cluster) still appear in tile gallery, visibly present but marked "Coming Soon," with a one-line description from the Five Lenses' Describe content. Tapping one shows what it'll do and, where relevant, why it's waiting (needs self-hosted infrastructure, tracked via Section 9.0).

Root Settings placement: Every category's settings screen ends with a "Root Settings" subsection, always visible (never hidden) but grayed out and non-interactive on an unrooted device.

Settings tile does NOT open one giant screen — it opens a category list (Network, Privacy, Firewall, Device, Appearance, Advanced...) exactly like stock Android Settings' drill-down pattern.

Not bound by true Android home-screen widget size limits since this grid lives inside our own app — only real ceiling is screen height, handled by internal scrolling on tall tiles.

8.4 Home Screen Widget Customization Studio (Gemini.md 4.3)

The Widgets management module provides a dedicated studio for configuring Android home screen widgets:



Categorized Selection List: Displays all available app features with individual checkboxes and a master "Select All" toggle at the top of each category column.

Visual Styling Controls:

Themes: Light, Dark

Shapes: Square, Round, Rounded Square

Background Styles: Solid, Transparent (Glassmorphism), Hidden

8.6 Newbie/Expert Mode (CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf)

Newbie Mode: Simplified interface with essential tiles only.

Expert Mode: Full access to all features and advanced settings.

Caregiver View (F167): Large-text, high-contrast layout; trusted contact can get limited remote view/assist permission (with subject's consent) to help troubleshoot without full device access.

10.1 Google Play Interception Features



ID



Name



Pri



Diff



Cost



Source



F96

Per-App Google Isolation

P1

3

L

Claude

Description: Automates the manual "force-stop and disable Google Play Services" workaround. One-tap per app: (1) Force-stop and disable GMS, (2) Launch game, (3) Re-enable GMS automatically once game is up (or when closed). Most users won't need root.

Legal Note: Only for stopping Google's background services from blocking apps you legitimately have. Cannot and will not bypass paid-license checks.

Related Features:



F169 Number-Loss Guard: Checklist and automated check across installed messaging apps, confirming two-step/PIN verification is turned on, warning if not.

F170 VoIP Number Directory: Crowd-sourced picker listing which free/cheap VoIP or eSIM services currently pass verification for which apps.

F171 SMS Forwarding Relay: If you still have brief access to a number before it's cut off, auto-forward incoming verification codes to new number or email.

10.5 Future Tech Considerations (From CLAUDEchatFULL.pdf)



Concept



Relevance



Action



Wi-Fi 7 Multi-Link Operation (MLO)

Simultaneous multi-band connections

Failover chain (F158) and traffic tools should treat as one logical link

5G Network Slicing

Apps can request prioritized data "slice"

Tile showing which apps are using it

Android's Private DNS and per-profile DNS

Stay aligned with Android changes

DoH/DoT switcher (F125) keeps working

Passkeys replacing passwords

Future of authentication

On-device AI (Gemini Nano)

Local pattern-matching

Future home for F03/F60's work, running fully offline

Thread/Matter smart-home networking

New local mesh protocol

See and firewall smart-home devices same way as phone apps

UWB becoming standard

Precision device-finding

Strengthens F146

eSIM-only phones

No physical SIM swapping

Makes F149 more important

16.2 State.md Build Order (Tier-Based, Server-Dependency Ordered)

Principle: Four real levels, ordered No-to-Yes:



No dependency — fully on-device, works instantly.

Public free infrastructure — Tor network, public DNS resolvers, GitHub.

Self-hosted — coordination server, relay, requires someone to stand up.

Costs money — required paid service. Confirmed: Nothing in this register requires Level 4.

Reorganization: Old alphabetical Phases A–R replaced with tiers ordered by priority-then-difficulty, using each feature's existing Pri and Diff ratings.



Within any tier: Build Diff-1/2 items before Diff-4/5 items. Diff column is the tiebreaker.



Structural Guarantee: Every feature is an independent module reachable only through the Module SDK interface (Module Contract 4.2.11). A Tier 9 module can depend on a Tier 1 module's interface, but nothing in an earlier tier is ever modified to accommodate a later one.

17.3 QA Philosophy

Automated unit tests (Module Contract 4.2.5) catch regressions; they don't prove a feature is good enough for daily use.



Before anything leaves Beta (F220) into Stable:



Needs real-device testing, not just CI green checks

Ideally from more than one device model (F243's compatibility map is the mechanism for gathering this)

"Stable" means: Used daily, on a real device, for at least one full week, with no crash and no silent failure

Nothing is rushed out of Beta to hit a date

18\. PARKING LOT — NOT FOR IMPLEMENTATION (Reference Only, Revisit When Ready)

Why this section exists: During the OS/root/de-Google discussion, a real fork-in-the-road question arose: how far to invest in the device-unlocking/OS-replacement path (root-dependent, higher effort, higher payoff for power users) versus staying focused on the no-root-friendly path (ADB/Shizuku, works for nearly everyone immediately, lower ceiling).



Nothing here gets built, scheduled, or referenced in planning until explicitly un-parked.

18.2 Technical Knowledge to Preserve

Android Treble Architecture:



System partition (OS/framework) and vendor partition (manufacturer drivers) are already separated by design, talking through a standardized interface called VINTF (Vendor Interface).

This is why GSIs (Generic System Images) work at all — a generic OS image never touches the vendor partition; drivers stay exactly as shipped.

Why Obscure Devices Still Take Forever Despite Treble:



Not every OEM implements VINTF cleanly — budget manufacturers especially pass Google's certification bar without polishing every driver interface, since no custom-ROM community was ever going to hold them accountable.

This is compatibility-testing labor, not driver-writing labor, and it's discovered empirically per device, not derivable by diffing source code (the vendor binaries are closed; there's no AOSP source to diff against).

The Two Real Technical Blockers (Confirmed from Real Device-Support Threads):



VINTF/vendor interface mismatches — hardware pieces (Wi-Fi, RIL/cellular, camera, fingerprint) come up broken or need hand-written workarounds even on devices where a GSI successfully boots.

Verified Boot / vbmeta signature chain — root + unlocked bootloader grant permission to flash something different; they do NOT automatically disable the Verified Boot signature check. That's a separate, explicit step (disabling or replacing vbmeta) that has to be done correctly, and getting it wrong is a documented cause of bootloops.

LineageOS's Actual Real-World Process (Confirmed Correct):



Pull stock firmware

Extract proprietary binary blobs (drivers/firmware, not modified-AOSP source to diff)

Write a device tree describing the hardware

Compile against those extracted pieces

Legal Grounding: This is legally grounded in reverse-engineering exemptions for interoperability — fundamentally different, legally, from defeating encryption or copying protected logic.



The Hard Legal/Technical Line, Never to Be Crossed:



Legal: Extracting and reusing a manufacturer's binary driver (interoperability) is categorically different from defeating Verified Boot's cryptographic signature chain (illegal under DMCA 1201 in the US and equivalent laws elsewhere, regardless of device ownership).

Every feature in this parking lot was scoped to stay on the legal side of that line.

A direct request to cross it was declined during conversation and should continue to be declined if raised again — this isn't a gap in capability, it's a boundary that holds regardless of framing.

The Doogee V Max Specific Situation:



Budget/niche devices like this typically ship with locked, non-unlockable bootloaders and no official unlock method — that's the manufacturer's business decision, not a Google restriction.

It's the actual reason this path wasn't available, not lack of user knowledge.

One Doogee model (Y8, not V Max) does have some community Treble GSI overlay support, suggesting Doogee as a brand isn't totally absent from this community — a mildly encouraging data point for later.

What Already Ships Regardless of This Decision:



F310 (ADB debloat, no root) and F321 (Shizuku layer) are not parked — they work today, for nearly everyone, with no bootloader unlock, no risk, and stay the flagship first-touch feature of the device-freedom category.

18.3 Watch-and-Revisit Note: REA and Reverse-Engineered GMS

The REA (Reverse Engineer Anything) project is a real, legitimate, MIT-licensed general tool (AI-agent-assisted analysis via Ghidra/JADX/Apktool) — fine to integrate into F212's Developer Console as a generic capability.



Separately: Google's own GMS license explicitly prohibits reverse engineering GMS itself, so a project that decompiles and redistributes Google's actual service code (as opposed to microG's clean-room reimplementation of documented API behavior) sits on the risky side of the DMCA interoperability line (Section 4.12).



Action: Nothing concrete has shipped from any "REA community GMS reimplementation" as of this writing — revisit if/when something real and clearly clean-room ships; until then, F315 (microG) remains the legally solid path and is NOT parked.



18.4 New Context: Android OS Build Verification (Research Sweep, Batch 11)

Android 17 is introducing native OS verification specifically to help users confirm their device runs an official build, explicitly designed to detect "modified Android versions that appear legitimate while secretly compromising security," rolling out first on Pixel.



Impact: This is a real, new obstacle for the paused custom-GSI/de-Google path (F311-F320): a legitimately de-Googled AOSP build could potentially get flagged by this mechanism depending on how it's implemented.



Action: Worth researching the specifics of how this verification works (what it actually checks) before this section is ever un-parked, since it may change the calculus.
